* [Comparing Contrasting Cases](comparing-contrasting-cases.md) - Learners study two or more deliberately varied cases side by side and articulate the differences, making deep structural features visible before instruction.
* [Comparing Multiple Solution Methods](comparing_multiple_solution_methods.md) - Learners compare and contrast multiple solution methods for the same problem, as opposed to studying one method at a time.
* [Compassionate Curiosity](compassionate_curiosity.md) - Act as a non-judgmental investigator to better understand students and respond with empathy.
* [Competency Based Learning](competency-based-learning.md) - Learners advance by demonstrating mastery of defined competencies rather than by accumulating seat time, with assessment and pacing tied to evidence of proficiency.
* [Competency Based Progression](competency-based-progression.md) - Learners advance through a curriculum by demonstrating mastery of defined competencies rather than by seat time or course completion.
* [Complete Scripts of Academic Routines](complete_scripts_of_academic_routines.md) - Provide scaffolding, like speech examples, for discourse routines that may not be obvious to learners.
* [Completion Problems](completion-problems.md) - Learners are given a partially solved problem and must complete the remaining steps, bridging worked examples and independent problem solving.
* [Completion Problems First](completion-problems-first.md) - Learners begin with partially completed problems (completion problems) before moving to full problem solving, bridging worked examples and independent practice.
* [Complex Instruction](complex_instruction.md) - A pedagogy that redefines who is 'smart' by utilizing multi-ability access points, norms and roles that support interdependency between learners, and attention to status and accountability for learning.
* [Composition Projects Multimodal](composition_projects-multimodal.md) - Learners create original compositions that combine multiple modes — text, image, audio, video, spatial design — to demonstrate understanding and communicate to real audiences.
* [Compound Word Study](compound_word_study.md) - Begin teaching how to spell multisyllable words by beginning with compounds.
* [Comprehension Checks](comprehension_checks.md) - During reading activities, instructors periodically check for understanding by asking targeted questions.
* [Comprehension Monitoring](comprehension_monitoring.md) - This strategy involves the ability of readers to know when they understand what they read and to use strategies to improve their understanding when it is blocked.
* [Comprehensive School Physical Activity Programs](comprehensive-school-physical-activity-programs.md) - A whole-school framework that provides students with multiple opportunities for physical activity before, during, and after school.
* [Concentric Circles](concentric_circles.md) - Learners form two facing circles (or lines) and rotate through brief structured peer discussions, meeting a new partner for each question.
* [Concept Discovery Through Examples and Non-examples](concept_discovery_through_examples_and_non-examples.md) - Expose learners to a wide range of examples and non-examples and allow them to discover the concept through guided comparison and classification.
* [Concept Mapping](concept_mapping.md) - Concept maps are visual tools that help learners organize and articulate what they know about a given concept or topic.
* [Concept Probing](concept-probing.md) - Asking diagnostic questions that surface learners' prior conceptions, intuitions, and misconceptions before or during instruction.
* [Conceptual Change](conceptual-change.md) - Instruction designed to replace learners' intuitive but inaccurate prior conceptions with scientifically accurate ones, typically by surfacing misconceptions and creating cognitive conflict.
* [Concrete Examples](concrete-examples.md) - Grounding abstract concepts, principles, or procedures in specific, tangible instances that learners can examine before generalizing.
* [Concrete Representational Abstract](concrete-representational-abstract.md) - A graduated instructional sequence that moves learners from hands-on concrete materials to visual representations to abstract symbols.
* [Concrete Representational Abstract (CRA) Sequence](concrete-representational-abstract_cra_sequence.md) - A graduated instructional sequence that moves learners from hands-on concrete materials, to visual representations, to abstract symbols of the same concept.
* [Concrete Representational Abstract Sequence](concrete-representational-abstract-sequence.md) - A three-phase instructional sequence that moves learners from hands-on concrete materials, through visual representations, to abstract symbols.
* [Concrete Representational Abstract Sequencing](concrete-representational-abstract-sequencing.md) - Teach a concept first with physical or concrete materials, then with visual representations, then with abstract symbols, fading across stages as understanding consolidates.
* [Concrete-Representational-Abstract (CRA)](concrete-representational-abstract_(cra).md) - CRA is an instructional approach that uses concrete materials, visual representations, and abstract symbols to teach math concepts.
* [Conferencing](conferencing.md) - One-on-one or small-group conversations between instructor and learner used to give individualized feedback, diagnose understanding, and plan next steps.
* [Confidence Based Quizzing](confidence-based-quizzing.md) - Learners answer quiz items and simultaneously rate their confidence, with scoring or feedback adjusted to reward accurate self-assessment as well as correct answers.
* [Conflict De Escalation](conflict_de-escalation.md) - A set of verbal and behavioral techniques for lowering the intensity of interpersonal conflict so that learning can resume and relationships are preserved.
* [Confront Fears](confront_fears.md) - A strategy in which learners identify, decompose, and systematically approach feared situations or tasks, reducing avoidance and building self-efficacy through graduated exposure and cognitive reappraisal.
* [Connect Content To Student Lives](connect_content_to_student_lives.md) - Linking academic content to students' personal experiences, cultural backgrounds, and communities so that new material feels relevant and meaningful.
* [Connect Math to Student Strengths](connect_math_to_student_strengths.md) - Help learners understand how math is used in ways that relate to their personal strengths by relating math to student interests.
* [Connect Prior Knowledge to New Learning](connect_prior_knowledge_to_new_learning.md) - Building connections to prior understandings and experiences helps learners integrate new information into their existing knowledge base.
* [Connect with Parents Early in the School Year](connect_with_parents_early_in_the_school_year.md) - Connecting with parents as soon as possible in the school year through personalized phone calls, emails, in-person conversations, or letters home.
* [Connect Writing to Real-World Applications](connect_writing_to_real-world_applications.md) - Instructors connect the importance and usefulness of writing to learners' lives and future careers.
* [Connecting](connecting.md) - Connecting involves relating a current problem or text to prior knowledge and experiences so new material is understood as a meaningful extension of what learners already know.
* [Connecting Background Knowledge To Content](connecting_background_knowledge_to_content.md) - A method for making instruction more culturally relevant by connecting content to learners' experiences.
* [Connecting Concepts to Procedures](connecting_concepts_to_procedures.md) - Instruction explicitly links conceptual understanding with procedural skill so each reinforces the other, rather than teaching algorithms as rote steps.
* [Connecting ELLs' Background Knowledge to Content](connecting_ells'_background_knowledge_to_content.md) - This strategy involves making instruction more culturally relevant by connecting content to ELLs' experiences.
* [Connecting Emotions to Actions](connecting_emotions_to_actions.md) - Learners reflect on how emotions influence their actions, then discuss and plan alternative behaviors.
* [Connecting Languages](connecting_languages.md) - Encourage students to see connections between their languages to better understand how languages are structured and organized.
* [Connecting Math to ESOL](connecting_math_to_esol.md) - Integrating authentic mathematical content and numeracy tasks into English for Speakers of Other Languages (ESOL) instruction so learners build language proficiency through meaningful quantitative contexts.
* [Connecting New Vocabulary to Texts](connecting_new_vocabulary_to_texts.md) - Integrating vocabulary instruction directly with texts so new words are learned in the context where they carry meaning.
* [Connecting Struggles to Strategies](connecting_struggles_to_strategies.md) - Explicitly teaching students a repertoire of problem-solving strategies and helping them recognize when, why, and how to deploy each one when they get stuck.
* [Connection of Prior Knowledge to New Learning](connection_of_prior_knowledge_to_new_learning.md) - Building connections to prior understandings and experiences to facilitate new learning.
* [Consider Socio-Cultural Factors](consider_socio-cultural_factors.md) - Acknowledge that access to technology and digital literacy can vary based on socio-economic background, social environment, and cultural factors, and design accordingly.
* [Consider the News Source](consider_the_news_source.md) - Learners evaluate who is behind a news report — its ownership, editorial practices, sourcing, and accountability structures — before judging its credibility.
* [Consider Your Own Identity](consider_your_own_identity.md) - Educators reflect on their own racial identity, biases, and privileges before and while leading discussions about race, so that self-awareness rather than unexamined assumption guides facilitation.
* [Consistent Classroom Routines and Schedules](consistent_classroom_routines_and_schedules.md) - Maintaining predictable classroom routines and schedules provides students with a sense of security and predictability, which supports emotional regulation and maximizes time on task.
* [Consolidate Key Points](consolidate_key_points.md) - Summarizing and consolidating key points after pair talk helps to solidify understanding and ensure that all students have a clear takeaway.
* [Constructive Decision-Making Process](constructive_decision-making_process.md) - Co-constructing and displaying an explicit decision-making process as a visual reference that students use to structure choices, evaluate consequences, and reflect on outcomes.
* [Consult Fact-Checking Sites](consult_fact-checking_sites.md) - Learners regularly use professional fact-checking services and reverse image search to verify claims and media before accepting or sharing them.
* [Contacts Between Students And Faculty](contacts-between-students-and-faculty.md) - Frequent, meaningful, and varied interactions between students and instructors that build engagement, belonging, and intellectual mentorship.
* [Content Word Signal](content_word_signal.md) - Using a content \"word of the week\" to signal that it's time for silence involves selecting a term related to the current curriculum (e.g., integer, renaissance, circuit) and using it as a cue for students to become quiet.
* [Content-Focused Questioning](content-focused_questioning.md) - Asking students questions that focus on the main points of a text and how the author supports those points with evidence and examples.
* [Content-Related Signal Word](content-related_signal_word.md) - Using a content \"word of the week\" to signal that it's time for silence.
* [Context-Specific Journaling](context-specific_journaling.md) - Context-specific journaling requires individuals to reflect on specific experiences such as team assignments, internships, or professional practices.
* [Contextual Redefinition](contextual-redefinition.md) - Learners encounter an unfamiliar word in a rich sentence context, hypothesize its meaning, then receive confirmation or correction to refine the definition.
* [Contextual Vocabulary Introduction](contextual_vocabulary_introduction.md) - Introducing vocabulary words within the context of a story or reading passage so learners derive meaning from surrounding text rather than from isolated definitions.
* [Contract Grading](contract_grading.md) - An assessment arrangement in which instructor and learner agree in advance on the work, quality criteria, and grade attached to specified levels of completed work.
* [Contrasting Cases](contrasting-cases.md) - [one-line]
* [Conver-Stations](conver-stations.md) - A small-group discussion strategy in which learners rotate between evolving groups, carrying ideas with them to broaden exposure to peers' perspectives.
* [Conversations about Emotions and Social Issues](conversations_about_emotions_and_social_issues.md) - Structured, explicit conversations in which learners name, discuss, and reason about emotions and social issues, including issues of social justice.
* [Cooperative Classroom Activities](cooperative_classroom_activities.md) - Design classroom activities that involve cooperative work rather than competitive or individualistic work.
* [Cooperative Learning](cooperative-learning.md) - Structured small-group work in which learners pursue shared goals under conditions of positive interdependence and individual accountability.
* [Cooperative Learning](cooperative_learning.md) - Structuring small-group work so that learners pursue shared goals under conditions of positive interdependence and individual accountability.
* [Cooperative Learning Activities](cooperative_learning_activities.md) - Structured group activities in which learners work interdependently toward a shared goal, with individual accountability and positive interdependence built into the task design.
* [Cooperative Learning Strategies](cooperative_learning_strategies.md) - Using structured group activities with positive interdependence and individual accountability to enhance learning and social interaction.
* [Cooperative Teaching](cooperative_teaching.md) - Structuring learning activities so students work together and teach each other concepts and skills, deepening understanding through explanation and peer support.
* [Correcting Students In A Constructive Way](correcting_students_in_a_constructive_way.md) - Addressing student errors or misbehavior in a manner that preserves dignity, targets the behavior rather than the person, and builds toward self-regulation.
* [Corroboration](corroboration.md) - Learners verify a claim by checking it against multiple independent sources before accepting or sharing it.
* [Countdown Clock / Visual Analog Timer](countdown-clock-visual-analog-timer.md) - A visual timer that represents elapsed and remaining time as a shrinking colored field, making abstract time durations perceptible and chunkable.
* [Countdown Technique](countdown_technique.md) - A classroom management technique in which the teacher counts down from a number (e.g., \"I need two people… you know who you are. I need one person…\") to prompt students to quiet down and refocus.
* [Counting All](counting-all.md) - A foundational early-arithmetic strategy in which learners solve addition or subtraction problems by counting every object in the combined set from one, rather than computing from a known sum.
* [Counting Based Arithmetic Strategies](counting-based-arithmetic-strategies.md) - Learners solve arithmetic problems by counting procedures — counting all, counting on, or counting up/down — rather than retrieving facts from memory.
* [Counting Circles](counting_circles.md) - A whole-class routine in which learners count aloud around a circle by a chosen increment from an arbitrary starting number, building number sense, fluency, and flexible mental computation.
* [Counting Collections](counting-collections.md) - Learners count real collections of objects in self-chosen ways, then record and discuss their counting strategies to build number sense and place-value understanding.
* [Counting On](counting-on.md) - A mental arithmetic strategy in which learners start from a given number and count forward by the addend rather than counting both quantities from one.
* [Cover Copy Compare](cover-copy-compare.md) - A self-managed practice procedure in which learners study a model, cover it, reproduce it from memory, and immediately compare their response to the model.
* [CRAAP Test](craap-test.md) - A checklist-based rubric (Currency, Relevance, Authority, Accuracy, Purpose) that students apply to score the credibility of a website or source.
* [CRAAP Test](craap_test.md) - A checklist-based rubric (Currency, Relevance, Authority, Accuracy, Purpose) that guides learners through evaluating the credibility of a source such as a website.
* [Cramming](cramming.md) - Massing study into a single session immediately before an assessment, relying on short-term familiarity rather than durable retention.
* [Create a Calm-Down Corner](create_a_calm-down_corner.md) - Set aside a dedicated, positive reflection space in the classroom where kids can go to practice their emotional regulation skills.
* [Create a Character Profile](create_a_character_profile.md) - Creating a character profile helps students form a deeper understanding of a book's characters through structured analytical and creative activities.
* [Create a Classroom Code of Conduct](create_a_classroom_code_of_conduct.md) - Teachers and students collaborate to establish a classroom code of conduct based on respectful, fair, kind, and empathetic behaviors.
* [Create a List of Positive Self-Talk Statements](create_a_list_of_positive_self-talk_statements.md) - Generating a collection of affirmative statements for learners to read, personalize, and internalize as a resource for managing negative thoughts.
* [Create a Word Wall for Emotions](create_a_word_wall_for_emotions.md) - Build students' emotion vocabulary by giving them a persistent, visible reference of feelings words they can use to identify, label, and discuss their emotional states.
* [Create Beautiful Spaces](create_beautiful_spaces.md) - Transforming classrooms and schools into beautiful environments that display high-quality student work, signaling that students' thinking and effort are valued.
* [Create Culture Kits](create_culture_kits.md) - Students assemble collections of personally meaningful objects representing their cultural backgrounds, then share and discuss them to build intercultural understanding and classroom community.
* [Create Space for Students to Reflect](create_space_for_students_to_reflect.md) - Providing structured opportunities for students to pause, reflect, and think about themselves, their ideas, and their feelings about projects can transform a classroom culture.
* [Creating a Classroom Library](creating_a_classroom_library.md) - Setting up a classroom library involves acquiring books and materials, organizing the shelves, creating labels, and making the space cozy.
* [Creating a Common Language for Multimodal Composition](creating_a_common_language_for_multimodal_composition.md) - Empowering students with a shared vocabulary to analyze and discuss the multimodal composition process.
* [Creating a Culture of Error](creating_a_culture_of_error.md) - Fostering a learning environment where mistakes are treated as information and learning opportunities rather than failures, so learners attempt, disclose, and analyze errors openly.
* [Creating a Digital Library Section for SEL Topics](creating_a_digital_library_section_for_sel_topics.md) - A digital library includes a dedicated section of books focused on social and emotional learning (SEL) topics.
* [Creating A Sense Of Belonging](creating_a_sense_of_belonging.md) - Designing learning environments so learners feel accepted as valued members of the learning community, which raises motivation, engagement, and persistence.
* [Creating a Strengths-Based Culture](creating_a_strengths-based_culture.md) - A method for empowering learners by focusing on their strengths rather than their weaknesses, creating authentic learning experiences that enable them to develop skills and talents.
* [Creating An Action Plan](creating_an_action_plan.md) - Learners convert a goal into a concrete, sequenced plan specifying when, where, and how they will act, and how they will monitor progress.
* [Creating Beautiful Spaces in PBL](creating_beautiful_spaces_in_pbl.md) - Displaying and celebrating high-quality student work from projects throughout the school building to honor effort, build pride, and transform the environment into an inspiring learning space.
* [Creating Collaborative Spaces](creating_collaborative_spaces.md) - Designing the physical and social environment so that group work, peer interaction, and joint problem-solving are the default mode of learning rather than the exception.
* [Creating Questions](creating_questions.md) - Learners generate their own questions about what they are hearing, at structured pauses during listening, to promote active comprehension and metacognitive monitoring.
* [Creating Quiet Spaces](creating_quiet_spaces.md) - Designating areas in the learning environment where learners can retreat for individual reflection, focused work, or relaxation.
* [Creating Space for New Ideas](creating_space_for_new_ideas.md) - Schools provide structured opportunities, resources, and psychological safety for teachers to try new approaches, take risks, and learn from failure.
* [Creating Space for Student Reflection in PBL](creating_space_for_student_reflection_in_pbl.md) - Integrating deliberate pauses for students to examine their thinking, feelings, and project progress, converting experience into durable learning.
* [Creating Strategy Charts](creating_strategy_charts.md) - Developing visual aids and lists of problem-solving strategies in collaboration with learners.
* [Creating Visuals](creating_visuals.md) - When students express information visually, they activate more cognitive processes while problem solving and gain experience with alternate representations.
* [Creative Analogies and Comparisons](creative_analogies_and_comparisons.md) - Simplify complex ideas by linking them to familiar situations, making them easier to understand and remember.
* [Creative Check-In Questions](creative_check-in_questions.md) - Instructors open a session with an unexpected, thought-provoking question that invites reflection and emotional awareness before academic work begins.
* [Creative Handwriting Activities](creative_handwriting_activities.md) - Engaging, multi-sensory writing activities that move away from traditional pencil-and-paper drill to build letter formation, fluency, and positive attitudes toward writing.
* [Creative Presentations](creative_presentations.md) - Visual presentation design that uses color, imagery, and layout to direct attention and support comprehension — effective only when decoration serves, rather than competes with, the content.
* [Creative Writing](creative_writing.md) - Using storytelling and creative writing tasks to deepen, consolidate, and personalize learning of subject-matter content.
* [Critical Friends Protocol](critical-friends-protocol.md) - A structured, facilitated peer-feedback protocol in which colleagues examine each other's work through defined roles, timed steps, and norms that separate warm feedback from judgment.
* [Critical Lens Development](critical_lens_development.md) - Learners analyze how different modes of communication (image, text, layout, sound) afford or constrain meaning, building evaluative criteria they apply to their own multimodal compositions.
* [Critical Response Process](critical_response_process.md) - The Critical Response Process is a structured method for giving feedback that puts the learner in control.
* [Critique And Revision](critique-and-revision.md) - Learners evaluate work (their own or others') against criteria, then use that evaluation to produce an improved version.
* [Critique Protocol](critique-protocol.md) - A structured procedure for giving and receiving feedback on work-in-progress, so that peer critique is specific, actionable, and separated from judgment of the maker.
* [Critique Protocols](critique-protocols.md) - Structured routines that guide learners to give and receive specific, kind, and useful feedback on work-in-progress.
* [Critique Protocols](critique_protocols.md) - Structured routines for giving and receiving feedback on work-in-progress, making peer critique specific, kind, and useful.
* [Cross Age Tutoring](cross-age-tutoring.md) - Older students tutor younger students, with the tutor gaining from teaching and the tutee from individualized instruction.
* [Cross-Age Peer Tutoring](cross-age_peer_tutoring.md) - Cross-age peer tutoring pairs students from different grade levels, with older students acting as tutors for younger students, so that both tutor and tutee benefit academically and socially.
* [Cross-Curricular Art Integration](cross-curricular_art_integration.md) - Integrating art-making with other subjects such as science, history, and mathematics so learners express and consolidate academic content through artistic production.
* [Cross-Grade-Level Tutoring](cross-grade-level_tutoring.md) - Form cross-grade-level alliances where older learners tutor those in lower grades.
* [Cultivate Multiple Ways of Knowing and Making Meaning](cultivate_multiple_ways_of_knowing_and_making_meaning.md) - Honoring multiple and diverse ways of developing understandings of the world ensures that learners have opportunities to connect with the material in ways that resonate with their individual strengths and backgrounds.
* [Cultivate Understanding and Respect Across Languages and Dialects](cultivate_understanding_and_respect_across_languages_and_dialects.md) - Using translations, descriptions, movement, and images to support learning in unfamiliar or complex languages promotes understanding and respect for diverse linguistic backgrounds.
* [Cultivating Belonging Through Environmental Cues](cultivating_belonging_through_environmental_cues.md) - Uses cues in the physical and social environment — representation, language, and signaling — to communicate to all learners that they belong in this space.
* [Cultivation of Multiple Ways of Knowing and Making Meaning](cultivation_of_multiple_ways_of_knowing_and_making_meaning.md) - Honoring multiple and diverse ways of developing understandings of the world.
* [Cultivation of Understanding and Respect Across Languages and Dialects](cultivation_of_understanding_and_respect_across_languages_and_dialects.md) - Using translations, descriptions, movement, images, and multilingual resources to support comprehension in unfamiliar languages while fostering respect for linguistic diversity.
* [Cultural Exchange Kits](cultural_exchange_kits.md) - Students curate and present a personal collection of artifacts that represent their culture, then exchange and discuss kits to build intercultural understanding.
* [Cultural Responsive Practice](cultural_responsive_practice.md) - Culturally responsive practice engages students in the learning process through an understanding of how their identity and perspective is shaped by their culture and community experiences.
* [Culturally and Historically Responsive Literacy](culturally_and_historically_responsive_literacy.md) - An equity framework that plans literacy instruction around four learning goals — identity, skills, intellect, and criticality — using texts and tasks responsive to students' cultures and histories.
* [Culturally Competent Family Engagement](culturally_competent_family_engagement.md) - Culturally competent family engagement involves training school staff in behaviors, attitudes, and policies that enable effective cross-cultural relationships.
* [Culturally Responsive Curriculum Scorecards](culturally_responsive_curriculum_scorecards.md) - Rubric-based evaluation tools, developed with educators, families, and students, for scoring how well curricula reflect diverse cultural backgrounds and perspectives.
* [Culturally Responsive Instruction (CREATE Model)](culturally_responsive_instruction_(create_model).md) - This strategy entails providing instruction that empowers students intellectually, socially, emotionally, and politically by using cultural referents to impart knowledge, skills, and attitudes.
* [Culturally Responsive Practice](culturally_responsive_practice.md) - Engaging learners cognitively and emotionally by understanding how their identity and perspective are shaped by culture and community experiences.
* [Culturally Responsive Teaching](culturally-responsive-teaching.md) - Teaching that engages students cognitively and emotionally by connecting instruction to their cultural identities, community experiences, and funds of knowledge to promote equity and belonging.
* [Culturally Responsive Teaching](culturally_responsive_teaching.md) - Culturally responsive teaching engages students cognitively and emotionally by using their cultural identities, languages, and community experiences as assets in instruction.
* [Culture Responsive Instruction](culture_responsive_instruction.md) - Teaching that makes standards-based content accessible by connecting it to students' cultural backgrounds, prior knowledge, language, and lived experience.
* [Cumulative Quizzing](cumulative-quizzing.md) - Distributing review questions across time so that each quiz includes material from prior units, not just the most recent content, exploiting retrieval practice and spacing to build durable memory.
* [Cumulative Review](cumulative-review.md) - Systematically revisiting previously learned concepts and skills throughout a course so that retention is maintained and new learning connects to old.
* [Cumulative Review](cumulative_review.md) - Cumulative review systematically revisits previously learned concepts and skills throughout a course, integrating older material into current activities rather than teaching topics in isolated blocks.
* [Cumulative Review Quizzes](cumulative-review-quizzes.md) - Low-stakes quizzes that interleave items from current and all prior units, exploiting retrieval practice and distributed spacing to build durable retention.
* [Cumulative Review Quizzing](cumulative-review-quizzing.md) - Structuring quizzes and low-stakes tests so that each one includes items from earlier units, not just recent material, exploiting the testing effect and spacing to combat forgetting.
* [Curated Material Selection](curated_material_selection.md) - Regularly assessing instructional materials for relevance and currency, discarding broken or obsolete items, and selecting new resources against explicit learning criteria.
* [Curating Resources](curating_resources.md) - Selecting, organizing, and annotating a deliberately limited set of learning materials so learners spend effort on learning rather than searching, evaluating, and triaging content.
* [Curriculum Based Measurement](curriculum-based-measurement.md) - Brief, frequent, standardized probes drawn from the year's curriculum used to monitor student progress and inform instructional decisions.
* [Cursive Handwriting Activities](cursive_handwriting_activities.md) - Structured activities for teaching and practicing cursive handwriting, supporting pencil control, letter formation, and writing fluency.
* [Customizable Display of Information](customizable_display_of_information.md) - Providing adjustable settings and accessible formats to accommodate individual needs and preferences.
* [Customizing Display of Information](customizing_display_of_information.md) - Customizing the display of information involves modifying the appearance of digital content to reduce reading effort and improve comprehension.

#### D {: #letter-d }

* [Daily 5](daily-5.md) - A classroom management and literacy framework in which students rotate through five purposeful independent literacy tasks while the teacher delivers small-group or individual instruction.
* [Daily Behavior Report Cards](daily-behavior-report-cards.md) - A daily rating of a student's behavior targets, communicated to the student and home, used to monitor, reinforce, and improve classroom behavior.
* [Daily Class Schedules](daily_class_schedules.md) - Structuring the school day with specific time allocations for subjects and activities to maximize instructional time and match demands to student energy and attention.
* [Daily Math Facts Practice](daily_math_facts_practice.md) - Beginning each math lesson with short, spaced practice sessions to build automatic retrieval of arithmetic facts.
* [Daily Review](daily_review.md) - Daily review is a strategy where instructors begin lessons with a short (5-10 minute) review of previously studied material.
* [Data Driven Dialogue](data-driven-dialogue.md) - A structured, facilitator-led conversation protocol in which educator teams move through predictable phases — predicting, observing, and inferring — to explore student data before jumping to conclusions or action.
* [Data Driven Inquiry](data-driven-inquiry.md) - A collaborative cycle in which educators analyze student learning data to identify gaps, hypothesize causes, and adjust instruction.
* [Data Driven Instruction](data-driven-instruction.md) - Using ongoing evidence of student learning to select, adjust, and differentiate instruction rather than relying on pacing guides or intuition.
* [Data-Based Decision-Making for Core Instruction](data-based_decision-making_for_core_instruction.md) - Teachers systematically collect, analyze, and act on student performance data to adjust Tier 1 (whole-class core) instruction.
* [Daytime Blue Light Exposure](daytime_blue_light_exposure.md) - Exposure to blue-enriched light (sunlight, full-spectrum LEDs, blue-enriched white lamps) during the day increases alertness and supports cognitive performance by entraining the circadian system, particularly for adolescents with delayed sleep-wake cycles.
* [De-emphasize Answers, Emphasize Reasoning](de-emphasize_answers,_emphasize_reasoning.md) - Spend more time on reasoning than on finding the correct answer.
* [De-emphasize Behavioral Factors](de-emphasize_behavioral_factors.md) - Remove non-academic criteria such as behavior, effort, and participation from grades so that grades reflect only academic mastery of learning goals.
* [Dealing with Feelings](dealing_with_feelings.md) - A structured self-reflection strategy in which adults learn to recognize, express, and self-soothe the emotions that arise when they make mistakes, compensating for emotional guidance they did not receive earlier in life.
* [Debate](debate.md) - Learners argue assigned positions on a contested question, responding to opposing arguments under structured rules.
* [Debate for the Opposite Team](debate_for_the_opposite_team.md) - Learners are assigned to argue a position that is opposite to their own, requiring them to see things from a different perspective.
* [Debriefing](debriefing.md) - A structured reflective conversation after an experience (simulation, case, discussion, or task) that converts doing into durable learning.
* [Debriefing Emotional Experiences](debriefing_emotional_experiences.md) - A structured end-of-day discussion in which each learner names an emotion they experienced, shares its context, and hears peers do the same.
* [Decodable Text Reading](decodable-text-reading.md) - Beginning readers practice with texts deliberately restricted to phonics patterns already taught, so decoding—not guessing—is the primary word-reading strategy.
* [Decodable Text Reading](decodable_text_reading.md) - Having beginning readers practice with texts whose words are restricted to letter–sound patterns already taught, so decoding rather than guessing is the primary reading strategy.
* [Decodable Text Reading Practice](decodable-text-reading-practice.md) - Practice reading texts deliberately written so that most words contain only phonics patterns already taught, allowing learners to apply decoding skills rather than guess.
* [Decodable Texts](decodable-texts.md) - Texts deliberately restricted to grapheme–phoneme correspondences and high-frequency words that students have already been taught, so early readers can decode rather than guess.
* [Decoding Drills](decoding-drills.md) - Short, repeated, timed practice exercises in which learners apply letter–sound correspondences and blending routines to read words accurately and automatically.
* [Deliberate Play](deliberate-play.md) - Structured yet intrinsically motivating play activities designed to develop skills and enjoyment simultaneously, typically in early-stage skill development.
* [Deliberate Practice](deliberate_practice.md) - Deliberate practice involves focused, strategic efforts to improve specific skills or knowledge areas.
* [Demonstrating Caring](demonstrating_caring.md) - Demonstrating caring communicates sincere personal investment in learners through everyday actions and words, building the relational trust that underpins motivation, persistence, and classroom climate.
* [Demonstration](on-the-job_training_(ojt).md) - ...
* [Demonstration-Based Flipped Classroom](demonstration-based_flipped_classroom.md) - Instructional demonstrations (often screencasts) are assigned as pre-class homework, freeing class time for guided practice, feedback, and problem-solving.
* [Demystification of Learning Challenges](demystification_of_learning_challenges.md) - Explaining to learners how memory, attention, and learning work — especially when they struggle — so they can interpret difficulties accurately and adopt effective strategies.
* [Derived Facts](derived-facts.md) - Learners generate unknown facts from facts they already know, using reasoning strategies rather than rote recall.
* [Descriptive Feature Chart](descriptive_feature_chart.md) - A graphic organizer with horizontal and vertical axes used to build a structured descriptive model of an organism or object from direct observation.
* [Design at the Margins](design_at_the_margins.md) - Designing learning environments by centering the needs and experiences of students at the edges of a community, on the premise that solutions serving the most marginalized serve everyone.
* [Design Challenges](design-challenges.md) - Learners create an artifact or solution meeting specified criteria, driving learning through the decisions required to build it.
* [Design Thinking](design-thinking.md) - A learner-centered innovation process — empathize, define, ideate, prototype, test — used as a learning strategy in which students solve open-ended, human-centered problems through iterative cycles of making and feedback.
* [Designated Calm-Down Spot](designated_calm-down_spot.md) - Creating a designated calm-down spot is providing a safe and structured space within the classroom where students can go to regain control over their emotions.
* [Designing Personalized Learning for Engagement](designing_personalized_learning_for_engagement.md) - Designing personalized learning so that choices are flexible and accessible to all learners, pairing personalization with Universal Design for Learning (UDL) to maximize engagement across ability levels.
* [Desmos Classroom Activities](desmos-classroom-activities.md) - A strategy using Desmos Classroom's interactive, teacher-paced digital activities to make mathematical thinking visible, collect real-time student responses, and structure whole-class discussion.
* [Develop a Reliability Checklist](develop_a_reliability_checklist.md) - Learners collaboratively generate and apply a checklist of questions for evaluating the reliability of online sources.
* [Develop Cultural Awareness](develop_cultural_awareness.md) - Developing cultural awareness as an educator includes building empathy for diverse learners, intentionally recognizing how one's own identity intersects with learners' identities, and creating an awareness of how the environment can…
* [Develop Educator Understanding](develop_educator_understanding.md) - Educators build their own knowledge of self-awareness, learner variability, and the research behind them so they can interpret student behavior accurately and choose supports deliberately.
* [Develop Observable Criteria](develop_observable_criteria.md) - Using Bloom's Taxonomy, Webb's Depth of Knowledge, and Marzano's Dimensions of Learning to develop measurable course objectives that are concise, student-friendly, and clearly communicated.
* [Developing a Critical Lens for Multimodal Analysis](developing_a_critical_lens_for_multimodal_analysis.md) - Increasing students' awareness of effective and ineffective multimodal combinations by analyzing a mode's affordances and constraints.
* [Developing Cultural Awareness](developing_cultural_awareness.md) - An ongoing educator practice of building empathy for diverse learners, examining how one's own identity and biases shape instruction, and creating conditions where all learners experience a sense of belonging.
* [Developing Positive Classroom Pride](developing_positive_classroom_pride.md) - Instilling pride is a powerful way to develop positive teacher-learner relationships.
* [Diagnostic Assessment](diagnostic-assessment.md) - Assessment conducted before or at the start of instruction to identify learners' prior knowledge, misconceptions, and skill gaps so teaching can be targeted accordingly.
* [Diagnostic Pre Assessment](diagnostic-pre-assessment.md) - Assessing learners' prior knowledge, skills, and misconceptions before instruction to tailor teaching to actual starting points.
* [Diagram Similarities and Differences](diagram_similarities_and_differences.md) - Learners generate a list of similarities and differences between words or concepts and complete a Venn diagram or other comparison graphic organizer.
* [Dialogic Reading](dialogic_reading.md) - Dialogic reading is an interactive reading technique in which the adult helps the child become the teller of the story instead of passively listening.
* [Dialogue Journals](dialogue-journals.md) - An ongoing written exchange between learner and teacher in which both write regularly and respond to each other, creating a sustained, personalized conversation over time.
* [Dictation](dictation.md) - Learners transcribe spoken words, sentences, or passages, converting speech to written form to build spelling, phoneme-grapheme mapping, and word recognition.
* [Dictation And Encoding Practice](dictation-and-encoding-practice.md) - Learners write words or sentences from spoken input, forcing them to map sounds onto letters and retrieve spellings rather than merely recognize them.
* [Dictation And Spelling Practice](dictation-and-spelling-practice.md) - Learners write words or sentences from spoken input, then check and correct their spelling, building orthographic representations through retrieval and feedback.
* [Dictation Exercises](dictation-exercises.md) - Learners transcribe spoken language — writing down words, phrases, or sentences they hear — to build phoneme–grapheme mapping, listening accuracy, and automatic word recognition.
* [Dictation Software](dictation_software.md) - Dictation software allows learners to speak their ideas and have them transcribed into text, bypassing transcription demands during composition.
* [Dictation/Speech-to-text](dictation-speech-to-text.md) - Dictation, also known as speech-to-text or voice recognition, involves using technology to convert spoken words into written text.
* [Dictionary & Thesaurus](dictionary_&_thesaurus.md) - Incorporating web dictionaries and thesauruses into vocabulary lessons allows learners to quickly search and understand unfamiliar words.
* [Differentiated Instruction](differentiated-instruction.md) - Adjusting content, process, product, or learning environment based on assessed learner readiness, interest, and profile so all students can reach shared goals.
* [Differentiated Teaching](differentiated_teaching.md) - Differentiated teaching extends the knowledge and skills of every student in every class, regardless of their starting point, by adjusting content, process, products, or learning environment to learner readiness, interest, and profile.
* [Digital Literacy Instruction](digital_literacy_instruction.md) - Digital literacy instruction teaches learners to locate, evaluate, use, and create information with digital tools, combining technical skills with critical evaluation of online content.
* [Digital Noise Monitoring](digital_noise_monitoring.md) - Digital noise monitoring uses apps or devices to detect and signal excessive classroom noise, giving students real-time feedback to support self-regulation of the sound environment.
* [Digital Publishing](digital_publishing.md) - Digital publishing involves using online platforms and tools to share student writing and creations with a wider audience, fostering engagement and providing valuable feedback.
* [Digital Storytelling](digital_storytelling.md) - Digital stories are multimedia narratives that combine photographs, video, animation, sound, music, text, and a narrative voice to help learners construct and communicate knowledge.
* [Digital Tips for Quick and Thoughtful Peer Feedback](digital_tips_for_quick_and_thoughtful_peer_feedback.md) - Using digital tools to streamline the exchange of peer feedback so students give and receive timely, structured, and constructive comments on each other's work.
* [Digital Word Detectives](digital_word_detectives.md) - Students read e-books and use digital tools (dictionaries and notes) to support their development of vocabulary.
* [Digital Word Detectives — Building Vocabulary With E-Book Readers](digital_word_detectives-building_vocabulary_with_e-book_readers.md) - Learners read digital texts in e-book readers and act as "word detectives," using embedded dictionaries, highlighting, and annotation tools to encounter, investigate, and record unfamiliar words in context.
* [Dim or Natural Lighting](dim_or_natural_lighting.md) - Adjusting classroom lighting — dimming, tuning color temperature, or maximizing daylight — to support attention, mood, and sensory regulation.
* [Dimensions of Critical Evaluation](dimensions_of_critical_evaluation.md) - Explicitly teach learners the distinct dimensions used to evaluate information sources — relevance, accuracy, bias/perspective, and reliability — and how content-based and source-based judgments differ.
* [Direct Instruction](direct-instruction.md) - A teacher-led, explicitly structured instructional strategy in which skills are modeled, practiced, and assessed in small steps with high learner response rates and immediate feedback.
* [Direct Instruction (Online)](direct_instruction_(online).md) - Direct instruction in online learning delivers pre-developed, explicitly structured presentations and targeted feedback, with the instructor diagnosing misconceptions, clarifying concepts, and directing learners to further practice.
* [Direct Instruction Patterning](direct_instruction-patterning.md) - Sequencing and structuring instructional examples, rules, and practice so that learners induce the intended generalization with minimal error and maximal clarity.
* [Direct Instruction Phonics](direct_instruction-phonics.md) - Systematic, teacher-directed phonics instruction that explicitly teaches letter–sound correspondences and blending in a planned sequence so learners can decode unfamiliar words independently.
* [Direct Instruction Spelling](direct_instruction-spelling.md) - Explicit, systematic teaching of spelling patterns, high-frequency words, and error-correction strategies for commonly misspelled words.
* [Direct Instruction Web Based Reading Strategies](direct_instruction-web-based_reading_strategies.md) - Explicitly teaching the skills of locating, evaluating, synthesizing, and comprehending information on the web through modeled, guided, and independent practice.
* [Direct Instruction: Genre-specific Writing Strategies](direct_instruction-genre-specific_writing_strategies.md) - Explicitly teaching the characteristics, conventions, and strategies of a specific writing genre so students can read and write within it deliberately.
* [Direct Instruction: Keyword Search](direct_instruction-keyword_search.md) - Explicit teaching of how to select, combine, and refine search keywords — including synonyms, operators, and Boolean logic — alongside criteria for evaluating the credibility of retrieved sources.
* [Direct Instruction: Problem-Solving Strategies](direct_instruction-problem-solving_strategies.md) - Instructors explicitly teach and model a repertoire of problem-solving strategies, then give learners supported opportunities to choose which strategies to apply and reflect on the outcomes of their choices.
* [Discipline-Specific Literacy](discipline-specific_literacy.md) - Discipline-specific literacy involves explicitly teaching the reading, writing, speaking, and thinking practices unique to each subject area, rather than treating literacy as a generic, transferable skill.
* [Discipline-Specific Reading](discipline-specific_reading.md) - Have students read authentic articles from subject-specific publications so they internalize the genres, vocabulary, and reasoning conventions of the discipline.
* [Discuss the Benefits of Positive Self-Talk](discuss_the_benefits_of_positive_self-talk.md) - Openly communicating with learners what self-talk is, how it works, and how it can improve persistence, performance, and emotional regulation.
* [Discussing Emotions](discussing_emotions.md) - Incorporating structured discussions and reflections about emotions into the learning environment so learners can identify, label, and manage the feelings that shape their learning.
* [Discussing Potential Emotions in Advance](discussing_potential_emotions_in_advance.md) - Learners anticipate and discuss the emotions a task, challenge, or transition may evoke before experiencing it, building emotional preparedness and regulation strategies.
* [Discussing Race With Students](discussing_race_with_students.md) - Structured classroom discussion of race and racism that moves beyond avoidance or colorblind platitudes toward evidence-based, emotionally supported dialogue.
* [Discussion (Pre-Reading)](discussion_(pre-reading).md) - A pre-reading activity in which learners physically commit to an opinion about the reading topic, build arguments for it, and debate peers holding opposing views before encountering the text.
* [Discussion Based Learning](discussion-based-learning.md) - Learners construct and refine understanding through structured dialogue with peers and instructors, rather than receiving knowledge primarily through presentation.
* [Discussion Norms](discussion-norms.md) - Explicitly established rules and expectations that govern how students participate in classroom discussion — who talks, how ideas are treated, and what counts as a good contribution.
* [Discussion Protocols](discussion-protocols.md) - Structured routines that govern how learners take turns, build on ideas, and reason together in discussion, replacing free-form conversation with explicit norms and sequences.
* [Discussion Protocols](discussion_protocols.md) - Structured routines that govern how learners take turns, build on ideas, and reason together during discussion, replacing free-for-all conversation with predictable participation structures.
* [Discussion Web](discussion_web.md) - A discussion web is a graphic organizer that enables students to examine both sides of an issue before agreeing on a conclusion, promoting deeper processing of texts through meaningful conversational discussion.
* [Discussion-Oriented Flipped Classroom](discussion-oriented_flipped_classroom.md) - First exposure to content happens through pre-class video (and other media) study; class time is devoted to facilitated discussion, debate, and collaborative analysis rather than lecture.
* [Discussions](discussions.md) - Engage learners in text-based discussions where they discuss what they read with each other, and teachers set expectations and routines for productive discussions.
* [Discussions to Guide Reflection](discussions_to_guide_reflection.md) - Structured discussions that prompt learners to articulate, examine, and evaluate their own thinking and learning processes.
* [Distributed Practice](distributed-practice.md) - Practicing content in short sessions spaced over time rather than massed into one long session, leveraging desirable difficulties to strengthen long-term retention.
* [Distributed Practice](distributed_practice.md) - Distributed practice involves practicing content in short sessions spaced out over time, contrasting with massed practice (cramming) where content is practiced in one long session.
* [Distributed Practice (Spaced Learning)](distributed_practice_(spaced_learning).md) - Distributed practice involves reviewing information or practicing skills across multiple sessions spread out over time, rather than in one continuous session (massed practice).
* [Distributed Practice (Spacing Effect)](distributed_practice_(spacing_effect).md) - Distributed practice schedules reviews of information or practice of a task across multiple sessions separated in time, rather than massing them together, producing markedly better long-term retention.
* [Diversity of Perspectives and Identities](diversity_of_perspectives_and_identities.md) - Incorporating diverse voices, experiences, and viewpoints into curriculum, materials, and discussion so learners encounter multiple ways of knowing and being.
* [Do Now / Bell Work](do_now_bell_work.md) - A short, self-directed task students begin immediately upon entering class, before instruction starts, used to activate prior knowledge, retrieve prior learning, or settle the room.
* [Document Outline](document_outline.md) - Using Google Docs' automatic document outline tool to have students mark main ideas and supporting evidence with headings, producing a live outline that scaffolds essay planning.
* [Document Outline Strategy](document_outline_strategy.md) - Using the “Document outline” tool in Google Docs, students pick out evidence that supports the main ideas they've found.
* [Document Outline Tool](document_outline_tool.md) - Learners plan and structure a document by drafting an outline — headings, sections, and key points — before writing prose, using a dedicated tool or template.
* [Dolch Sight Word List - Cloze Passage Worksheets](dolch_sight_word_list_-_cloze_passage_worksheets.md) - Grade-level cloze passage worksheets in which students supply missing Dolch sight words, building automatic recognition in meaningful context.
* [Dolch Sight Word List - Flash Cards](dolch_sight_word_list_-_flash_cards.md) - Sets of printable flash cards for Dolch sight words, organized by grade level, used for rapid recognition practice of high-frequency words.
* [Dolch Sight Word List - Word Shape Worksheets](dolch_sight_word_list_-_word_shape_worksheets.md) - Printable worksheets in which students identify and complete Dolch sight words using letter-shape (box) cues, building visual word recognition.
* [Dolch Sight Word Lists](dolch_sight_word_lists.md) - The Dolch Sight Word list comprises the 220 most common service words and 95 common nouns in children's texts, taught for instant recognition rather than decoding.
* [Dolch Word List - Word Search Puzzles](dolch_word_list_-_word_search_puzzles.md) - Word search puzzles built from Dolch sight words, used as low-stakes practice for automatic recognition of high-frequency words.
* [Domain Check](domain_check.md) - A quick source-evaluation heuristic in which students check the top-level domain of a website (.edu, .gov, .org, .com) as a first-pass indicator of credibility.
* [Don’t Jump Into "Fix It" Mode](don’t_jump_into_"fix_it"_mode.md) - Resist the urge to immediately find solutions; listen and understand first, then collaborate on what support the learner actually wants.
* [Don’t Say My Number](don’t_say_my_number.md) - A counting game where a chosen number is forbidden, and learners must skip it while counting aloud in sequence.
* [Dot Card Flash](dot_card_flash.md) - One learner flashes a dot card to another for about 3 seconds, then hides it; the second learner recreates the arrangement from memory.
* [Dot Card Matching Games](dot_card_matching_games.md) - Learners match dot cards with equal quantities but different arrangements, or match dot cards to corresponding numeral cards, building subitizing and number-sense foundations.
* [Double-Entry Journals](double-entry_journals.md) - Learners split a page into two columns, recording quotations or text excerpts on one side and their personal responses, questions, or interpretations on the other as they read.
* [Doubles Minus One](doubles-minus-one.md) - A derived-fact strategy for addition in which learners solve near-double facts (e.g., 6+5) by using a known doubles fact (6+6) and adjusting down by one.
* [Doubles Plus One](doubles-plus-one.md) - A derived-fact strategy for addition in which learners solve near-double facts (e.g., 7+8) by using a known doubles fact (7+7) and adding one.
* [Drafting And Feedback Cycles](drafting-and-feedback-cycles.md) - Learners produce successive versions of a work product, with feedback between drafts used to guide revision rather than to grade the final attempt.
* [DragonBox Algebra 5+ App](dragonbox_algebra_5+_app.md) - A game-based app that teaches algebraic reasoning by having learners manipulate cards to isolate a "box," implicitly solving equations before formal notation is introduced.
* [Dramatic Play](dramatic_play.md) - Dramatic play (pretend play) is a learning strategy in which learners take on imagined roles and act out scenarios, experimenting with social, emotional, and linguistic roles through imagination.
* [Draw Time When Felt Emotion](draw_time_when_felt_emotion.md) - Learners draw pictures of a time when they felt specific emotions and share with classmates.
* [Draw To Explain](draw-to-explain.md) - Learners construct drawings or diagrams to explain a concept, process, or system, using generative visual construction rather than viewing provided images.
* [Drawing and Memory Enhancement](drawing_and_memory_enhancement.md) - Drawing is a powerful technique to enhance memory and recall by engaging learners in multiple cognitive processes.
* [Drawing and Writing Developmental Stages](drawing_and_writing_developmental_stages.md) - Understanding the developmental stages of drawing and writing can help educators tailor activities to match a child's current abilities.
* [Drawing for Early Writing and Reading Development](drawing_for_early_writing_and_reading_development.md) - Drawing is a strategy to foster creativity, planning, and the development of foundational literacy skills in young learners.
* [Drawing for Emergent Writing](drawing_for_emergent_writing.md) - Drawing activities support emergent writing by giving young learners unstructured drawing time and using their drawings as plans and prompts for early literacy tasks.
* [Drawing for Formative Assessment](drawing_for_formative_assessment.md) - Inviting learners to express their understanding through drawings provides insights into their comprehension and any misconceptions.
* [Drawing for Memory and Comprehension](drawing_for_memory_and_comprehension.md) - Learners create their own drawings of to-be-learned content, forcing generative visual, kinesthetic, and semantic processing that improves recall and understanding.
* [Drawing for Memory and Learning](drawing_for_memory_and_learning.md) - Learners create drawings of to-be-learned content as an encoding strategy, producing better memory than writing or other study methods.
* [Drawing To Learn](drawing-to-learn.md) - Learners generate drawings or diagrams to construct and organize understanding of content, rather than only reading or viewing prepared visuals.
* [Dual Coding](dual-coding.md) - Pairing verbal information with complementary visual representations so learners encode content through two channels and build richer, better-connected memory structures.
* [Dual Color Highlighting](dual_color_highlighting.md) - Dual color highlighting is a text-to-speech feature that highlights the word being read aloud in one color while highlighting the containing sentence in another, synchronizing auditory and visual input during reading.
* [Dynamically Introduce Academic Vocabulary](dynamically_introduce_academic_vocabulary.md) - Use repeated encounters with a word in varied, authentic contexts — anchored by a memorable first encounter — to help learners internalize definitions and use words flexibly.

#### E {: #letter-e }

* [E.S.C.A.P.E. Junk News](e.s.c.a.p.e._junk_news.md) - A handy acronym to help students remember six key concepts for evaluating information.
* [Easy-Access Educational Tools](easy-access_educational_tools.md) - Making educational tools readily available to students supports self-advocacy by fostering independence and choice.
* [Echo Reading](echo-reading.md) - The teacher reads a short segment of text aloud with fluent expression, and learners immediately re-read the same segment aloud, imitating the model.
* [Education Calendars](education_calendars.md) - Education calendars are visual aids designed to keep students, parents, and teachers informed about important dates, events, and activities.
* [Educational Charts](educational_charts.md) - Educational charts are visual aids that present data in an accessible format, revealing patterns and stories behind the numbers.
* [Educational Infographics](educational_infographics.md) - Educational infographics compress complex information into a single visual artifact combining charts, icons, and concise text to support comprehension and recall.
* [Educational Posters](educational_posters.md) - Educational posters are persistent visual displays in the learning environment that reference key concepts, strategies, or resources.
* [Educational Research Map Visualization](educational_research_map_visualization.md) - Using an interactive bibliometric map to explore, navigate, and make sense of education and learning sciences research.
* [Effective Feedback](effective_feedback.md) - Feedback is any response from a teacher regarding a student's performance or behavior, provided with the goal of closing the gap between current and desired performance.
* [Effective Materials Management](effective_materials_management.md) - Using an 'in' and 'out' basket system for papers, labeling cubbies, bookshelves, bins, and folders, and providing easily accessible supplies cuts down on student time locating materials and supports organization.
* [Effort-Based Praise](effort-based_praise.md) - Praising learners' efforts and strategies—as opposed to their intelligence—cultivates a growth mindset and enhances motivation and performance.
* [Elaborative Interrogation](elaborative-interrogation.md) - Learners generate explanations for why a stated fact or concept is true, prompting integration of new material with prior knowledge.
* [Electricity and Circuit Tasks](electricity_and_circuit_tasks.md) - Electricity and circuit tasks involve investigating how electricity flows in simple circuits using tools like electric testers and puzzle boards.
* [Eliminate Extra Credit](eliminate_extra_credit.md) - Remove extra credit opportunities so that all grades reflect demonstrated mastery of the required curriculum rather than access to supplemental time, money, or resources.
* [Eliminate Group Grades](eliminate_group_grades.md) - Replacing shared group grades with individual accountability structures so that collaborative work does not mask unequal contribution or learning.
* [Eliminate Homework and Extra Credit](eliminate_homework_and_extra_credit.md) - Remove homework completion and extra credit from final grades, treating practice as ungraded learning activity and reserving grades for evidence of mastery.
* [Eliminate Internal Negative Chatter](eliminate_internal_negative_chatter.md) - Becoming aware of negative self-talk and actively restructuring it through thought-stopping, cognitive reappraisal, and replacement with constructive inner dialogue.
* [Embrace Errors as Learning Opportunities](embrace_errors_as_learning_opportunities.md) - Treating errors as sites for analysis and discussion rather than failures to be avoided, so that wrong answers surface misconceptions and drive conceptual change.
* [Embracing Errors as Learning](embracing_errors_as_learning.md) - A strategy that reframes errors as productive sites of learning by treating mistakes as diagnostic information, analyzing them openly, and building a classroom culture where error-making is safe and expected.
* [Emotion Charades](emotion_charades.md) - Emotion Charades involves a student acting out a scenario that makes them feel a certain way, while the rest of the group guesses the emotion being portrayed.
* [Emotion Check Ins](emotion-check-ins.md) - Brief structured activities at the start of a session or task in which learners identify and name their current emotional state, building self-awareness and giving instructors actionable information about readiness to learn.
* [Emotion Cue Cards](emotion_cue_cards.md) - Portable visual or auditory prompts that remind students of self-selected strategies for managing emotions, such as deep breathing, using a fidget, or taking a mindfulness break.
* [Emotion Emojis](emotion_emojis.md) - Students express how they are feeling by drawing or sharing an emoji, then optionally explain their choice to peers.
* [Emotion Emojis Check-In](emotion_emojis_check-in.md) - Students express their current feelings by drawing or sharing an emoji that represents their emotional state.
* [Emotion Guessing Game](emotion_guessing_game.md) - An emotion guessing game involves an instructor or student making an emotion face, and the others guess the emotion being expressed.
* [Emotion Hello Song](emotion_hello_song.md) - Singing a greeting song with varied emotional expressions so learners identify, label, and share emotions at the start of a session.
* [Emotion Identification Activities](emotion_identification_activities.md) - Activities that help learners recognize, label, and express emotions in themselves and others, building emotional literacy and self-awareness.
* [Emotion Identification and Labeling](emotion_identification_and_labeling.md) - Learners identify, label, and match facial expressions and feelings to build emotional literacy.
* [Emotion Identification Games](emotion_identification_games.md) - Games and playful exercises in which learners recognize, name, and act out emotions to build emotional literacy and social understanding.
* [Emotion Identification through Literature](emotion_identification_through_literature.md) - Uses emotion posters during read-alouds to help students infer and name a character's emotions from language and pictures.
* [Emotion Password](emotion_password.md) - Learners make a prescribed facial expression (the "password") to enter the classroom, prompting brief emotion recognition and self-assessment at the threshold of the lesson.
* [Emotion Reflection Through Journaling](emotion_reflection_through_journaling.md) - Learners reflect on experiences, tracking patterns in their emotions and behaviors to enhance self-understanding and goal-setting.
* [Emotion Songs](emotion_songs.md) - Emotion songs involve creating or adapting songs to explore, label, and express different emotions.
* [Emotion Vocabulary Building](emotion_vocabulary_building.md) - Explicitly teaching learners a differentiated lexicon for emotions so they can identify, label, and communicate internal states with precision.
* [Emotion Word Inclusion in Instruction](emotion_word_inclusion_in_instruction.md) - Educators deliberately name emotions in their own speech — labeling their own feelings, the learner's feelings, and characters' feelings — to build learners' emotional vocabulary and model healthy emotional communication.
* [Emotion-Based Story Discussions](emotion-based_story_discussions.md) - Emotion-based story discussions involve reading stories and having learners identify and discuss the characters' feelings.
* [Emotion-Focused Pretend Play](emotion-focused_pretend_play.md) - An adult joins children's pretend play and introduces emotion talk — naming, modeling, and acting out how the imaginary characters feel — to build emotional understanding and expression.
* [Emotional Check-ins](emotional_check-ins.md) - A brief, structured routine at the start of a session in which learners report their current emotional state, giving the educator early insight into readiness to learn and individual needs.
* [Emotional Regulation Strategies](emotional_regulation_strategies.md) - Educators identify students' strengths and challenges in emotional regulation—the ability to control responses to emotional stimuli—and align supports and instruction to those profiles.
* [Emotional Thermometer](emotional_thermometer.md) - An emotional thermometer is a visual self-assessment tool that helps learners identify and gauge the intensity of their emotions so they can apply coping strategies before escalation.
* [Emotional Vocabulary Building](emotional_vocabulary_building.md) - Learners are provided direct access to emotion words, using a word wall or chart to expand their emotional vocabulary.
* [Emotional Walking Game](emotional_walking_game.md) - Learners walk through a space and shift their posture, gait, and expression to embody a named emotion each time the facilitator signals.
* [Empathic Discipline](empathic-discipline.md) - A brief online intervention that reframes discipline as a relationship-preserving act, reducing suspensions by shifting teachers' mindset from punitive to empathic responses to misbehavior.
* [Empathic Discipline](empathic_discipline.md) - A discipline approach that frames misbehavior as an opportunity to understand the student's perspective and maintain the relationship, rather than to punish.
* [Empathy for Educators](empathy_for_educators.md) - Engage in deliberate actions to build an empathic mindset toward students, using perspective-taking, student voice, and reflective practice to inform instruction.
* [Empathy Walks](empathy-walks.md) - Learners physically or imaginatively walk through a situation from another person's perspective, then reflect on the experience to build empathic understanding.
* [Emphasis on Reasoning over Answers](emphasis_on_reasoning_over_answers.md) - Shifting classroom attention from the correctness of final answers to the quality of reasoning processes, so learners build confidence and conceptual understanding even when their answers are wrong.
* [Emphasis on the Learning Process](emphasis_on_the_learning_process.md) - Helping learners focus on and value the process of learning, rather than just the grades they receive, promotes a growth mindset.
* [Emphasize Reasoning over Answers](emphasize_reasoning_over_answers.md) - De-emphasizing correct answers and spending more time on reasoning involves shifting the focus from finding the right solution to understanding the problem-solving process.
* [Emphasize the Value of Learning](emphasize_the_value_of_learning.md) - Help learners focus on and value the process of learning rather than solely on grades.
* [Emphasize Time on Task](emphasize_time_on_task.md) - Learning requires sufficient, well-allocated, and engaged time — there is no substitute for time on task.
* [Employ Common Language and Strategies Across Disciplines](employ_common_language_and_strategies_across_disciplines.md) - Aligning terminology, instructional routines, and writing expectations across subject areas so students encounter consistent skill language throughout the school day.
* [Empowering Students as Leaders](empowering_students_as_leaders.md) - Assign students with challenging behaviors structured leadership roles in collaborative groups, channeling their social energy into facilitation and peer support.
* [Empowering Students Through Self-Advocacy](empowering_students_through_self-advocacy.md) - Empowering students to advocate for themselves involves teaching them about their rights and needs, and providing them with opportunities to communicate and act on that understanding.
* [Empowering Teachers with EQUIP](empowering_teachers_with_equip.md) - EQUIP is a customizable classroom observation tool that quantifies patterns in student participation, empowering teachers to build more equitable classrooms through data-driven reflection.
* [Encourage Active Learning](encourage_active_learning.md) - Learning is not a spectator sport — learners must talk, write, apply, and reflect on material rather than passively receive it.
* [Encourage Cooperation](encourage_cooperation.md) - Learning is enhanced when it is more like a team effort than a solo race — learners articulate ideas, respond to others' thinking, and share responsibility for outcomes.
* [Encourage Feedback and Sharing](encourage_feedback_and_sharing.md) - Encouraging learners to share their work with peers and online fosters a collaborative writing environment and broadens the audience for their work.
* [Encourage Reading Diverse Texts](encourage_reading_diverse_texts.md) - Learners read diverse texts and then think and talk about different genres to acquire academic language.
* [Encourage Student Self-Advocacy](encourage_student_self-advocacy.md) - Empowering students to become self-advocates involves helping them understand their strengths and challenges, and providing them with a voice in their learning.
* [Encouraging Help-Seeking Behavior](encouraging_help-seeking_behavior.md) - Creating a classroom environment where students feel comfortable asking for help.
* [Encouraging Self Advocacy](encouraging_self-advocacy.md) - Self advocacy involves understanding one's own needs, articulating what supports would help, and communicating those needs to others to build independence and problem-solving capacity.
* [Encouraging Student Self-Advocacy](encouraging_student_self-advocacy.md) - Empowering learners to communicate their needs, understand their rights, and actively seek support.
* [Encouraging Students to Ask for Help](encouraging_students_to_ask_for_help.md) - Creating classroom conditions and teaching routines that normalize and skillfully support adaptive help-seeking when students struggle.
* [Engage Positive Role Models](engage_positive_role_models.md) - Involving families, community members, and other credible models in classroom and school activities so learners can observe and identify with successful performance.
* [Engage with Empathy](engage_with_empathy.md) - Engaging with empathy means communicating in online communities in ways that demonstrate understanding of others' perspectives — reading carefully, responding thoughtfully, and upholding community norms.
* [Enhanced Acoustics and Noise Control](enhanced_acoustics_and_noise_control.md) - Designing and retrofitting classrooms to buffer external noise and control reverberation, protecting speech intelligibility and attention.
* [Enriched Learning Environment](enriched_learning_environment.md) - Creating a brain-friendly and enriched classroom environment involves optimizing the learning space by considering layout, furniture choices, color selections, lighting, nature, environmental print, and organization of materials.
* [Enrichment Through Art and Music](enrichment_through_art_and_music.md) - Teachers integrate art and music from diverse cultures into their lessons to enrich learning, engage students, and promote cultural understanding.
* [Environmental Interaction Analysis](environmental_interaction_analysis.md) - These activities explore the relationships between people and their environments by examining environments, inhabitants, and the tracks they leave behind.
* [EQ Feeling Chart](eq_feeling_chart.md) - Pair students and check-in with each other using an EQ Feeling Chart.
* [EQ Feeling Chart Check-In](eq_feeling_chart_check-in.md) - Students use a provided EQ Feeling Chart to identify, label, and discuss their current emotions in pairs, then share observations as a class.
* [EQ Feeling Chart Exploration](eq_feeling_chart_exploration.md) - Learners use the EQ Feeling Chart to identify, name, and communicate their feelings with a partner, then share observations with the whole class.
* [Equitable Assessment Rubrics](equitable_assessment_rubrics.md) - Rubrics aligned to learning standards, objectives, and activities that illuminate student learning and mastery, with meaningful comments for formative assessment.
* [Equitable Calling on Students](equitable_calling_on_students.md) - Ensure all learners get response opportunities, not just high achievers.
* [Equitable Classroom Observations](equitable_classroom_observations.md) - EQUIP is a customizable observation tool for tracking patterns in student participation, aiming to empower teachers in building more equitable classrooms.
* [Equitable Grading](equitable_grading.md) - Equitable grading redesigns grading policies and practices so that grades accurately reflect learning rather than behavior, timing, or bias.
* [Equity Audits](equity-audits.md) - A systematic review of instructional designs, materials, and outcomes data to identify and remedy patterns that disadvantage particular learner groups.
* [Erroneous Examples](erroneous_examples.md) - Presenting deliberately flawed worked solutions for learners to diagnose, explain, and correct.
* [Errors as Learning Opportunities](errors_as_learning_opportunities.md) - Creating a classroom culture where errors become learning opportunities shifts the perception of mistakes as failures to opportunities for growth and understanding.
* [Essential Questions](essential-questions.md) - Open, generative questions that frame a unit or course, giving learners a purpose for inquiry and a conceptual throughline that connects individual lessons.
* [Establish Consistent Routines](establish_consistent_routines.md) - Use consistent routines and methods of giving instructions to help students know what is expected of them.
* [Establish Ground Rules for Peer Critique](establish_ground_rules_for_peer_critique.md) - Establishing ground rules for peer critique ensures that the process is productive, respectful, and beneficial for all learners.
* [Establish Group Norms](establish-group-norms.md) - Explicitly co-creating and reinforcing behavioral and interactional expectations that govern how a collaborative group communicates, makes decisions, and holds members accountable.
* [Establish Group Roles](establish-group-roles.md) - Assigning distinct, interdependent roles (e.g., facilitator, recorder, checker) to members of collaborative groups to structure participation and accountability.
* [Establish Guidelines for Discussing Race](establish_guidelines_for_discussing_race.md) - Establish guidelines or agreements for group members to make when having discussions of race to ensure respectful and productive dialogue.
* [Establish Guidelines for Discussions](establish_guidelines_for_discussions.md) - Establish clear norms for group discussion — engagement, comfort with discomfort, speaking one's truth, and accepting non-closure — before dialogue begins, especially on sensitive or controversial topics.
