* [Reflecting on Student Strengths and Challenges](reflecting_on_student_strengths_and_challenges.md) - Educators identify specific student strengths and challenges related to learning, social-emotional learning, or other domains, and use aligned strategies to leverage strengths to support learning and address challenges.
* [Reflection](reflection.md) - A deliberate, structured pause in which learners examine their own thinking, performance, or experience to consolidate learning and inform future action.
* [Reflection After Writing](reflection_after_writing.md) - Reflection involves pausing after completing a writing assignment to consider how the assignment went.
* [Reflection Exercises](reflection_exercises.md) - Reflection exercises are structured activities that prompt learners to examine their own experiences, feelings, and behaviors in order to build self-awareness, self-regulation, and deeper learning from experience.
* [Reflection On Learning](reflection_on_learning.md) - Structured prompts and activities that direct learners' attention to what they learned, how they learned it, and how it connects to prior knowledge and future application.
* [Reflection on Lessons](reflection_on_lessons.md) - Teachers systematically reflect on each lesson before and after teaching, recording what worked and what needs revision to improve future instruction.
* [Reflection on Student Learning](reflection_on_student_learning.md) - The teacher systematically assesses and reflects on each student's comprehension and engagement using critical thinking questions, observation, and productive struggle to inform subsequent instruction.
* [Reflection Prompts](reflection_prompts.md) - Reflection prompts are specific questions or statements used to guide learners in thinking deeply about their learning process, content understanding, and personal growth.
* [Reflection with Others](reflection_with_others.md) - Teachers improve their practice by collaboratively reflecting with peers — through non-evaluative observation, structured feedback conversations, and discussion of shared instructional problems.
* [Reflective Assessment](reflective_assessment.md) - Assessment designed so that grades and feedback reflect learning growth over time and prompt learner self-evaluation, rather than serving as terminal judgments of performance.
* [Reflective Journaling](reflective-journaling.md) - Learners regularly write structured reflections connecting course content to their own thoughts, experiences, and emotions to deepen understanding and develop reflective practice.
* [Reflective Journaling](reflective_journaling.md) - Reflective journaling is a technique used to encourage reflective learning by highlighting students' thoughts and perceptions about course content.
* [Reflective Journals](reflective-journals.md) - Learners regularly write structured entries connecting their experiences, actions, and learning to goals, theory, and future plans.
* [Reflective Practice](reflective-practice.md) - A structured process in which learners deliberately examine their own experiences, actions, and reasoning to extract lessons and improve future performance.
* [Reflective Prompts](reflective_prompts.md) - Structured questions that prompt learners to examine their learning experiences, processes, and outcomes, converting experience into articulated understanding.
* [Reflective Writing](reflective-writing.md) - Structured writing tasks that prompt learners to examine their own thinking, learning processes, and experiences in order to consolidate understanding and improve self-regulation.
* [Reflex Math App](reflex_math_app.md) - An adaptive, game-based app (ExploreLearning) that builds math fact fluency through individualized practice, timed retrieval, and progress monitoring for students in grades 2–8.
* [Reframe Anxiety as Excitement](reframe_anxiety_as_excitement.md) - A brief reappraisal technique that prompts learners to relabel anxious arousal as excitement before a high-stakes task, converting a threat mindset into an opportunity mindset.
* [Reframe Negative Self-Talk](reframe_negative_self-talk.md) - Practice reframing negative thoughts, statements, or situations into more positive alternatives.
* [Reframing Negative Self-Talk](reframing_negative_self-talk.md) - Reframing involves rephrasing negative thoughts, statements, or situations into more positive, accurate alternatives.
* [Reframing Self Talk](reframing-self-talk.md) - A strategy in which learners are taught to notice negative or unproductive internal dialogue and deliberately replace it with accurate, task-focused, or growth-oriented alternatives.
* [Refutation Texts](refutation-texts.md) - Texts that explicitly state a common misconception, refute it, and present the scientifically accepted alternative.
* [Regular Physical Education (PE) Classes](regular_physical_education_(pe)_classes.md) - Schedule consistent, quality physical education classes across the school week to support physical health, attention, and academic learning.
* [Regular Text Referencing](regular_text_referencing.md) - Regularly referring back to mentor texts in teacher conferences, whole-class lessons, or discussions reinforces learning and helps students internalize new techniques.
* [Reinforce Positive Behaviors](reinforce_positive_behaviors.md) - Recognizing and positively reinforcing desired actions when they are observed, using contingent praise and rewards to strengthen those behaviors.
* [Relate Math to Learner Strengths](relate_math_to_learner_strengths.md) - Help learners understand how math gets used in the world in ways that relate to their personal strengths.
* [Relate Math to Student Strengths](relate_math_to_student_strengths.md) - Connecting math content to students' existing interests, expertise, and out-of-school competencies so they enter instruction from a position of strength.
* [Relating Math to Student Strengths](relating_math_to_student_strengths.md) - This strategy leverages learners' existing strengths and interests to make math more relatable, engaging, and confidence-building.
* [Relationship Building](relationship-building.md) - Deliberate cultivation of trust, rapport, and mutual knowledge between instructor and learners (and among learners) to create the relational conditions for engagement, risk-taking, and persistence.
* [Relationship Skills Identification and Support](relationship_skills_identification_and_support.md) - Educators identify students' strengths and challenges in relationship skills and implement targeted, strengths-based supports to build communication, cooperation, and conflict management.
* [Relevant Vocabulary Instruction](relevant_vocabulary_instruction.md) - Instructors should present vocabulary that is useful and/or necessary for learners, based on their learning goals.
* [Remote Support with Augmented Reality (AR)](remote_support_with_augmented_reality_(ar).md) - AR apps allow technicians and users to communicate with experts in real time, with annotations overlaid on the user's field of view.
* [Remove Visual Distractions](remove_visual_distractions.md) - Strip unnecessary visual stimuli from the learning environment so attentional and working-memory resources are spent on the task rather than on irrelevant displays.
* [Repairing Harm](repairing_harm.md) - A restorative practice in which a student who has caused harm identifies the damage and stakeholders, then designs and carries out a plan to repair it, replacing exclusionary punishment with accountability and empathy-building.
* [Repeat Key Points](repeat_key_points.md) - Repeat the key points for students several times and in several different ways.
* [Repeat Key Points (Processing Speed)](repeat_key_points_(processing_speed).md) - Repeating key points several times and in several different ways to support learners with slower processing speed.
* [Repeated Reading](repeated-reading.md) - Learners reread the same passage multiple times until reaching a fluency criterion such as a target words-correct-per-minute rate.
* [Repeated Reading](repeated_reading.md) - Learners reread the same text multiple times until they reach a fluency criterion, building automatic word recognition that frees cognitive resources for comprehension.
* [Repeated Reading for Fluency](repeated_reading_for_fluency.md) - Learners reread the same passage multiple times until reaching a fluency criterion, building automatic word recognition that frees cognitive resources for comprehension.
* [Repeating Key Points in Different Ways](repeating_key_points_in_different_ways.md) - Repeating key points several times and in several different forms — rephrasing, examples, visuals, and varied modalities — reinforces understanding and gives learners additional processing time.
* [Repertoire of Strategies](repertoire_of_strategies.md) - Procedural fluency requires having a repertoire of strategies from which learners can flexibly choose.
* [Repetition](repetition.md) - Repeat key points multiple times in simple language so all learners get multiple exposures to core content.
* [Replace Negative Influences with Positive Ones](replace_negative_influences_with_positive_ones.md) - A self-management strategy in which learners identify sources of negativity in their social and informational environment and deliberately substitute them with supportive people, models, and content.
* [Representation of Diverse Perspectives and Identities](representation_of_diverse_perspectives_and_identities.md) - Incorporating multiple and varying perspectives within learning content to honor and value diverse approaches to learning.
* [Require Retakes](require_retakes.md) - Allowing or requiring retakes emphasizes a growth mindset and reinforces perseverance.
* [Rereading](rereading.md) - A literacy strategy in which a reader revisits a sentence, paragraph, or whole text to improve accuracy, fluency, vocabulary, and comprehension.
* [Research Map](research_map.md) - A research map is a visual representation of research topics and their interconnections, allowing users to explore academic literature in a structured and intuitive way.
* [Research Map Visualization](research_map_visualization.md) - A visual, interactive map of peer-reviewed education and learning sciences research, organized by bibliographic coupling into explorable topic clusters.
* [Research Reports](research_reports.md) - Research reports present data and findings in a visually structured, accessible format so that audiences beyond the research team can understand, evaluate, and act on the results.
* [Resistance Reading and Counter-Narratives](resistance_reading_and_counter-narratives.md) - Resistance reading and writing counter-narratives are strategies to develop learners' abilities to critique a text (or an image or other media) and to respond in writing, sharing their concerns and critiques.
* [Respect Diverse Talents and Ways of Learning](respect_diverse_talents_and_ways_of_learning.md) - There are many roads to learning.
* [Respectful Redirection](respectful_redirection.md) - Respectful redirection is a quick, in-the-moment strategy to give corrective feedback to students using a calm tone, neutral body language, and clear, concise wording.
* [Responding with \"Yet\"](responding_with_"yet".md) - Adding the word \"yet\" to responses when learners express frustration or inability (e.g., \"I can't do this\") introduces the idea of a growth mindset, fostering the belief that abilities can improve over time.
* [Response Cards](response-cards.md) - All learners simultaneously display answers on individual cards or boards, replacing one-at-a-time hand-raising with whole-group active responding.
* [Response Devices](response_devices.md) - Response devices allow learners to anonymously share answers with the whole class, enabling whole-group participation and rapid formative assessment.
* [Response To Intervention](response-to-intervention.md) - A multi-tier prevention framework that screens all learners, delivers escalating levels of targeted instruction, and uses continuous progress monitoring to decide when intensity should increase.
* [Responsible Decision-Making Practice](responsible_decision-making_practice.md) - Learners work through realistic social-ethical scenarios in pairs or small groups, analyze situations, generate and weigh decisions, and reflect on consequences and reasoning.
* [Restate The Question](restate_the_question.md) - Requiring learners to restate a question or problem in their own words before answering, building comprehension, slowing impulsive responding, and exposing misunderstandings early.
* [Restorative Approaches](restorative-approaches.md) - A relational discipline and community-building strategy that addresses harm through dialogue, accountability, and repair rather than exclusionary punishment.
* [Restorative Circles](restorative-circles.md) - A structured dialogue format in which a community addresses harm, conflict, or misbehavior by giving every participant an equal voice to share perspectives, name impacts, and agree on repair.
* [Restorative Circles](restorative_circles.md) - A structured, facilitated dialogue format in which community members affected by harm or conflict collaboratively name what happened, identify impacts, and agree on how to repair relationships.
* [Restorative Conversations](restorative-conversations.md) - A structured dialogue process in which participants affected by harm or conflict jointly explore what happened, who was affected, and how to repair it, replacing purely punitive responses.
* [Restorative Justice Conferencing](restorative-justice-conferencing.md) - A facilitated face-to-face meeting among those affected by harm — offender, victim, and community members — to acknowledge harm, take responsibility, and agree on repair.
* [Restorative Practices](restorative-practices.md) - A whole-community approach that builds relationships and repairs harm through facilitated dialogue rather than exclusionary punishment.
* [Restorative Practices](restorative_practices.md) - A relational approach to building classroom community and responding to harm through facilitated dialogue, replacing exclusionary discipline with accountability and repair.
* [Restorative Practices Circles](restorative-practices-circles.md) - A facilitated whole-group dialogue format in which participants pass a talking piece and respond to sequential prompts to build community, repair harm, or resolve conflict.
* [Restorative Questions](restorative-questions.md) - Open-ended questions that help learners reflect on harm, responsibility, and repair after conflict, replacing punitive discipline with dialogue.
* [Restorative Questions](restorative_questions.md) - Open-ended questions that shift responsibility for reflection and repair onto the learner after harm or conflict, replacing adult-imposed judgment with structured dialogue.
* [Retakes And Revisions](retakes-and-revisions.md) - Allowing learners to retake assessments or revise work after feedback, with the revised score replacing or improving the original, so assessment functions as a learning opportunity rather than a one-shot verdict.
* [Retelling](retelling.md) - Learners reconstruct a text, story, or event in their own words, making their comprehension visible and strengthening memory through generative recall.
* [Rethinking Accountability](rethinking_accountability.md) - Adjusting grading, assessment, and expectations so students are not unjustly penalized for circumstances beyond their control, while preserving meaningful standards.
* [Retrieval Practice](retrieval-practice.md) - Actively recalling information from memory — rather than re-reading it — to strengthen long-term retention and reveal knowledge gaps.
* [Retrieval Practice](retrieval_practice.md) - Actively recalling information from memory — rather than re-reading it — to strengthen long-term retention and understanding.
* [Retrieval Practice Activities](retrieval-practice-activities.md) - Activities that require learners to actively recall information from memory rather than re-read or re-hear it, strengthening long-term retention.
* [Retrieval Practice Openers](retrieval-practice-openers.md) - Opening a lesson or session with a short, low-stakes retrieval task on previously covered material to strengthen memory and set up new learning.
* [Retrieval Practice Training](retrieval-practice-training.md) - Explicitly training learners to use self-testing and recall-from-memory as a study strategy, rather than rereading or reviewing notes.
* [Retrieval Practice Warm Ups](retrieval-practice-warm-ups.md) - Opening a lesson with short, low-stakes retrieval of previously learned material to strengthen memory and activate prior knowledge.
* [Retrieval Practice With Graphic Organizers](retrieval_practice_with_graphic_organizers.md) - Students first recall everything they can about a topic from memory (a Brain Dump), then organize the retrieved material into a graphic organizer, optionally with a partner, and finally verify against source material.
* [Revision And Resubmission](revision-and-resubmission.md) - Learners act on feedback to improve and resubmit their work, treating the first attempt as a draft rather than a final judgment.
* [Revision Cycles](revision-cycles.md) - Structured cycles in which learners produce work, receive feedback, and revise it before final submission or publication.
* [Revision_Cycles](revision_cycles.md) - Structured cycles in which learners produce work, receive feedback, and revise it, treating improvement as an iterative process rather than a one-shot submission.
* [Rhyming Games](rhyming_games.md) - Rhyming games are playful, structured activities in which learners identify, match, and generate rhyming words to build phonological awareness as a foundation for reading and spelling.
* [Rhythmic Clapping/Counting](rhythmic-clapping-counting.md) - The teacher claps or counts out a rhythm pattern; students listen, then imitate it, building auditory discrimination, timing, and pattern recognition.
* [Rice Tables And Sandboxes Science Through Inquiry](rice_tables_and_sandboxes-science_through_inquiry.md) - Using sensory tables (rice, sand, water) as open-ended inquiry environments where young children investigate physical science concepts through manipulation, prediction, and observation.
* [Rich Library Audio & Braille](rich_library-audio_&_braille.md) - Providing learners with abundant, well-curated audio and braille versions of texts so that access to content does not depend on print reading.
* [Rich Library Complexity](rich_library-complexity.md) - Designing learning resources as a large, varied, multi-perspective library of cases and representations rather than a single streamlined sequence, so learners can criss-cross the knowledge landscape.
* [Rich Library: Multimodal](rich_library-multimodal.md) - Providing learners with a variety of resources in multiple formats — blogs, lyrics, videos, web pages, online discussions, fanfiction — to enhance disciplinary literacy, engagement, and access.
* [Rich Library: Multimodal Resources](rich_library-multimodal_resources.md) - Providing learners with access to multiple resources, including blogs, videos, audiobooks, and digital books, exposes them to diverse genres and can increase interest and motivation.
* [Rich Library: Poetry](rich_library-poetry.md) - Provides learners with a curated, browsable collection of poems and poetry-related multimedia resources to build literacy, engagement, and appreciation of poetic language.
* [Rich Resources: Diversity](rich_resources-diversity.md) - Curating learning materials that represent diverse perspectives, identities, and sources so learners deepen self-understanding and understanding of others.
* [RIP Toolbox (Repetition, Imagery, Patterns)](rip_toolbox_(repetition,_imagery,_patterns).md) - The RIP Toolbox is a memory-enhancing strategy that utilizes three key elements: Repetition, Imagery, and Patterns.
* [Role Assignment](role-assignment.md) - Assigning distinct, interdependent roles (e.g., facilitator, recorder, skeptic) to members of a collaborative group to structure participation and accountability.
* [Role Play](role-play.md) - Learners act out realistic scenarios in a safe environment, practicing communication, decision-making, and interpersonal skills with feedback before facing the real situation.
* [Role Play](role_play.md) - Learners act out assigned roles in a simulated scenario, practicing interpersonal skills and decision-making in a safe environment with structured feedback.
* [Role-Playing Emotional Scenarios](role-playing_emotional_scenarios.md) - Learners act out how to manage emotions in structured scenarios, connecting feelings to behaviors through rehearsal and guided reflection.
* [Role-Reversal 2.0 (Flipping the Teacher)](role-reversal_2.0_(flipping_the_teacher).md) - Students create instructional videos demonstrating their understanding, taking the teacher's role as explainer and producer of content.
* [Room Positioning](room-positioning.md) - Deliberately arranging where learners and the instructor sit or stand to support attention, interaction, and the intended mode of learning.
* [Routines](routines.md) - Repeated, predictable sequences of classroom or learning activity that automate procedural aspects of learning so attention and working memory are freed for content.
* [Routines And Signals](routines-and-signals.md) - Established, predictable procedures and attention cues that automate classroom transitions and reduce cognitive load, freeing attention for learning.
* [RSOWV/TRAP Mnemonics](rsowv-trap-mnemonics.md) - RSOWV (Relax, Slow down, Organize, Write, Visualize) and TRAP (Translate, Repeat, A Picture, Practice) are packaged mnemonic routines that structure encoding, rehearsal, and self-monitoring of to-be-learned material.
* [Rubric Based Assessment](rubric-based-assessment.md) - Assessment structured around explicit scoring criteria that describe performance levels, shared with learners before they attempt the task.
* [Rubric Based Feedback](rubric-based-feedback.md) - Using an explicit scoring rubric as the frame for feedback so learners see performance against named criteria rather than receiving unstructured comments.
* [Rubric Based Self Assessment](rubric-based-self-assessment.md) - Learners evaluate their own work against an explicit rubric, using the criteria as both a quality standard and a guide for revision.
* [Rubric Co Construction](rubric-co-construction.md) - Learners and instructor collaboratively develop the evaluation criteria and performance-level descriptions used to assess their work.
* [Rubric Feedback](rubric-feedback.md) - Providing learners with evaluative feedback structured around an explicit rubric that names criteria and quality levels, so feedback is criterion-referenced rather than impressionistic.
* [Rubric-Based Self Assessment](rubric-based_self-assessment.md) - Learners evaluate their own work against an explicit rubric, using the criteria as both a quality standard and a guide for revision.
* [Rubric_Co Construction](rubric_co-construction.md) - Learners work with the instructor to build or refine the evaluation criteria and quality definitions they will be assessed against.
* [Rubrics](rubrics.md) - Rubrics are evaluation tools consisting of a set of criteria, a fixed scale, and descriptors that distinguish the differences in the levels of the scale.
* [Running Records](running-records.md) - A standardized formative assessment in which a teacher codes a student's oral reading of a text in real time to analyze accuracy, fluency, and cueing behavior.

#### S {: #letter-s }

* [Safe and Healthy Elementary School Recess](safe_and_healthy_elementary_school_recess.md) - Implementing a safe and healthy recess with programs like Playworks TeamUp aims to build a positive school climate by promoting social, emotional, physical, and academic development.
* [Sail through Math App](sail_through_math_app.md) - A pirate-themed iOS app that builds arithmetic fluency in young learners through story-framed, leveled practice with immediate feedback.
* [Say it Once](say_it_once.md) - Deliver instructions a single time, then route follow-up questions to peers before the instructor, to build active listening and learner independence.
* [Scaffold Structure and Function](scaffold_structure_and_function.md) - Provide students with the language tools — sentence starters, stems, and frames — needed to succeed in project-based work by analyzing the linguistic demands of the project in advance.
* [Scaffolded Difficulty Progression](scaffolded-difficulty-progression.md) - Sequencing learning tasks from simple to complex, with support that fades as competence grows, so each task stays within the learner's zone of proximal development.
* [Scaffolded Instruction](scaffolded_instruction.md) - Scaffolded instruction supports learners with temporary, tailored assistance as they attempt tasks beyond their unaided capability, then gradually withdraws that support as competence develops.
* [Scaffolded Peer Review](scaffolded_peer_review.md) - Structured supports — exemplars, rubrics, scoped prompts, and modeled critique — that raise the quality of feedback learners give each other.
* [Scaffolded Practice](scaffolded-practice.md) - Practice arranged as a graded sequence of tasks with temporary supports that are progressively removed as learner competence grows.
* [Scaffolded Practice Sequences](scaffolded-practice-sequences.md) - A strategy that organizes practice tasks into a deliberate progression of increasing difficulty and decreasing support, so learners build competence without being overwhelmed or under-challenged.
* [Scaffolded Problem Sequencing](scaffolded-problem-sequencing.md) - Ordering practice problems from simple to complex with graduated support, so each task is slightly beyond current competence but within reach with available scaffolds.
* [Scaffolded Questioning](scaffolded-questioning.md) - A strategy in which the instructor sequences and adapts questions — from simple recall to increasingly complex reasoning — to guide learners through a zone of proximal development.
* [Scaffolding](scaffolding.md) - Temporary, tailored support that helps learners perform tasks beyond their current independent ability, then fades as competence grows.
* [Scaffolding for Self-Regulation](scaffolding_for_self-regulation.md) - Temporary, structured support that helps learners manage their emotional, motivational, and behavioral responses, gradually withdrawn as independent self-regulation develops.
* [Scaffolding in PBL](scaffolding-in-pbl.md) - Structured, temporary support embedded in problem-based learning to manage cognitive load and guide inquiry without eliminating productive struggle.
* [Scaffolding Self-Regulation](scaffolding_self-regulation.md) - Temporarily structuring tasks, modeling self-regulatory moves, and fading support so learners develop the ability to manage their own emotions, effort, and learning processes.
* [Scaffolding Structure and Function in PBL](scaffolding_structure_and_function_in_pbl.md) - Providing learners in project-based learning with explicit language structures (sentence starters, stems, frames) that scaffold both the form and the communicative function of academic discourse.
* [Scenario Based Training](scenario-based-training.md) - Learners practice decision-making and skill application inside a realistic, contextualized scenario that simulates the conditions of real performance.
* [Scenario-Based E-Learning](scenario-based_e-learning.md) - Learning tasks embedded in realistic contexts where learners make decisions, experience consequences, and receive feedback within a simulated situation.
* [Schema-Based Instruction](schema-based-instruction.md) - Teaching learners to recognize the underlying structure of problem types so they can map problem features to appropriate solution strategies.
* [Schema-Based Instruction](schema-based_instruction.md) - Schema-based instruction teaches learners to recognize the underlying structure of problems so they can map surface features to an appropriate solution strategy.
* [School Newsletters](school_newsletters.md) - School newsletters are recurring visual communications designed to keep staff, students, and parents informed and engaged with school events, activities, and learning priorities.
* [Schwa Instruction](schwa_instruction.md) - Explicit teaching of schwa (/ə/), the unaccented vowel sound whose identity is reduced, so learners can explain and predict why unstressed syllables are often not spelled the way they sound.
* [Science Notebooks](science-notebooks.md) - Students maintain an ongoing written record of observations, data, drawings, predictions, and explanations as they do science, using the notebook as both a learning tool and an artifact of scientific practice.
* [Science Through Inquiry](science_through_inquiry.md) - Engage learners in hands-on, exploratory science activities that encourage questioning, discovery, and testing to develop new understandings.
* [Screen Readers](screen_readers.md) - Screen readers convert on-screen text into synthesized speech or braille output, giving learners auditory (or tactile) access to written material.
* [Screencast Feedback](screencast-feedback.md) - Audiovisual feedback in which the instructor records their screen while narrating comments on a learner's work, combining spoken commentary with a visual walkthrough of the artifact.
* [Search Strategy Scaffolds](search-strategy-scaffolds.md) - Structured supports that teach learners how to formulate queries, select sources, and evaluate search results rather than leaving them to search by trial and error.
* [Secret Student](secret_student.md) - A classroom management system where a student's name is secretly selected each day, and the teacher observes whether the \"Secret Student\" exhibits good listening and learning behaviors.
* [Seductive Details Removal](seductive-details-removal.md) - Removing interesting but irrelevant text, images, audio, or video from learning materials to protect working memory and improve outcomes.
* [See, Think, Wonder](see,_think,_wonder.md) - A Project Zero thinking routine in which learners observe a stimulus and record what they see, what they think about it, and what it makes them wonder, surfacing observations, interpretations, and questions.
* [See, Think, Wonder](see-think-wonder.md) - A Project Zero thinking routine in which learners observe a stimulating image and record what they see, what they think about it, and what it makes them wonder.
* [See_Think_Wonder](see_think_wonder.md) - A visible-thinking routine that asks learners to describe what they observe, interpret what they think is happening, and pose questions about what they wonder.
* [Segmenting](segmenting.md) - Breaking continuous instructional material (especially animation, video, or narration) into learner-paced segments to manage cognitive load.
* [SEL Discussions on Ethical Dilemmas](sel_discussions_on_ethical_dilemmas.md) - This strategy involves using age-appropriate, real-life ethical dilemmas as discussion starters to help students practice responsible decision-making.
* [SEL Discussions through Literary Characters](sel_discussions_through_literary_characters.md) - Students read and discuss social and emotional learning (SEL) topics through the lens of characters in literature.
* [SEL Integration through Literature](sel_integration_through_literature.md) - Integrating social and emotional learning (SEL) into language arts curricula by using literature to explore emotions, build community, handle conflict, and counter bias.
* [SEL Questioning](sel_questioning.md) - Teachers use short, structured social-emotional learning (SEL) questions — focused on kindness, metaphors, challenges, and support — to grow students' emotional intelligence through individual reflection and class discussion.
* [SEL Videos with Discussion](sel_videos_with_discussion.md) - Showing short video clips depicting social and emotional situations, followed by structured discussion or related activities, to build learners' social-emotional understanding.
* [SEL-Focused Digital Libraries](sel-focused_digital_libraries.md) - Curating a digital library with a dedicated, searchable collection of books addressing Social-Emotional Learning (SEL) themes so teachers and students can integrate SEL into literacy instruction.
* [Selecting Culturally Responsive Texts](selecting_culturally_responsive_texts.md) - Selecting and incorporating texts that reflect learners' identities and expose them to authentic perspectives beyond their own experience.
* [Selecting Tiered Vocabulary Words](selecting_tiered_vocabulary_words.md) - Categorize vocabulary words into three tiers to prioritize instruction: Tier 1 (basic words, rarely require instruction), Tier 2 (high-frequency academic words, crucial for mature language), and Tier 3 (low-frequency, content-specific words).
* [Self Assessment Rubrics](self-assessment-rubrics.md) - Learners evaluate the quality of their own work against explicit, criterion-based rubrics, building evaluative judgment alongside the target skill.
* [Self Correction_Routines](self-correction_routines.md) - Structured procedures in which learners check, diagnose, and revise their own work against criteria before or instead of receiving external correction.
* [Self Evaluation](self-evaluation.md) - Learners assess their own work or performance against explicit criteria, building accurate self-monitoring alongside the target skill.
* [Self Explanation Prompting](self-explanation-prompting.md) - Prompts that ask learners to explain to themselves how new information relates to what they know, or why each step of a solution works, generating elaborations that improve understanding.
* [Self Explanation Prompts](self-explanation-prompts.md) - Prompts that ask learners to explain to themselves why a step, statement, or solution is correct, integrating new material with prior knowledge.
* [Self Monitoring](self-monitoring.md) - Learners deliberately track their own comprehension, performance, and progress against criteria during learning, feeding those judgments into adjustments of strategy and effort.
* [Self Monitoring Checklists](self-monitoring-checklists.md) - Learners use structured checklists to track their own performance, progress, or comprehension against explicit criteria during learning tasks.
* [Self Monitoring In Writing](self-monitoring-in-writing.md) - Learners deliberately observe, evaluate, and adjust their own writing processes and products against explicit criteria during planning, drafting, and revising.
* [Self Monitoring Of Performance](self-monitoring-of-performance.md) - Learners track and evaluate their own ongoing performance against standards or goals, using the resulting information to adjust effort, strategy, or approach.
* [Self Monitoring Strategies](self-monitoring-strategies.md) - Techniques that prompt learners to track their own comprehension, performance, and progress against criteria during learning.
* [Self Monitoring Strategies](self-monitoring_strategies.md) - Learners deliberately observe, record, and evaluate their own performance, attention, or comprehension during learning to regulate effort and strategy use.
* [Self Monitoring_Checklists](self-monitoring_checklists.md) - Learners use structured checklists to track their own progress, accuracy, and strategy use against explicit criteria during learning tasks.
* [Self Questioning](self-questioning.md) - Self questioning prompts learners to generate and answer their own questions about material, directing attention, deepening processing, and monitoring understanding.
* [Self Regulated Learning](self-regulated-learning.md) - A strategy in which learners actively plan, monitor, and control their own cognition, motivation, and behavior toward learning goals, supported by explicit instruction in these processes.
* [Self Regulated Learning Instruction](self-regulated-learning-instruction.md) - Explicitly teaching learners to plan, monitor, and control their own learning processes rather than relying on teacher-directed regulation.
* [Self Regulated Learning Strategy Instruction](self-regulated-learning-strategy-instruction.md) - Explicitly teaching learners the cognitive, metacognitive, and motivational strategies they need to plan, monitor, and evaluate their own learning.
* [Self Regulated Strategy Development](self-regulated-strategy-development.md) - SRSD is a structured instructional approach in which learners are explicitly taught academic strategies alongside the self-regulation skills—goal setting, self-monitoring, self-instruction, and self-reinforcement—needed to deploy them independently.
* [Self Talk](self-talk.md) - Self talk is the deliberate use of instructional or motivational statements directed at oneself — aloud or silently — to guide attention, regulate effort, and structure task performance.
* [Self Talk Logging](self-talk-logging.md) - Learners record their internal monologue — thoughts, confusions, decisions, and self-instructions — during learning tasks to make metacognitive processes visible and improvable.
* [Self Talk Scripts](self-talk-scripts.md) - Pre-scripted verbal prompts learners use to guide their own thinking, monitoring, and emotional regulation during a task.
* [Self- and Peer-Editing](self-_and_peer-editing.md) - Learners systematically evaluate and revise their own writing and provide structured feedback on peers' writing using checklists or rubrics.
* [Self-Awareness - Identify Feelings](self-awareness_-_identify_feelings.md) - Learners identify, label, and match facial expressions and feeling words to build the self-awareness component of social-emotional competence.
* [Self-Awareness - Journal Reflections](self-awareness_-_journal_reflections.md) - Learners regularly write reflective entries about their experiences, emotions, and outcomes to build self-awareness and self-regulation.
* [Self-Determined Learning Model of Instruction](self-determined-learning-model-of-instruction.md) - A coaching model in which students set, act on, and evaluate their own learning goals through a three-phase, twelve-step teacher-facilitated process.
* [Self-Evaluation in Writing](self-evaluation_in_writing.md) - Self-evaluation involves teaching students how to review their writing to decide if changes are needed.
* [Self-Instruction](self-instruction.md) - Learners deliberately talk themselves through a task — overtly at first, then covertly — using self-statements to direct attention, guide strategy use, cope with difficulty, and evaluate progress.
* [Self-instructions](self-instructions.md) - Teachers model articulating how they solve a problem and prompt students to explain their own thinking.
* [Self-Monitoring of Attention (SMA)](self-monitoring_of_attention_(sma).md) - Learners systematically observe and record their own attention-related behavior, using cues to prompt self-assessment at regular intervals.
* [Self-Monitoring of Behavior](self-monitoring-of-behavior.md) - Learners observe and record their own behavior (attention, compliance, performance), producing reactive improvements in the monitored behavior and building self-regulation.
* [Self-Monitoring of Behavior](self-monitoring_of_behavior.md) - Self-monitoring is a strategy where learners self-assess their behavior and record the results, increasing or decreasing the frequency, intensity, or duration of existing behavior.
* [Self-Monitoring of Performance (SMP)](self-monitoring_of_performance_(smp).md) - Self-monitoring of performance is a strategy where learners track an aspect of their own academic performance — such as rate of work completion or accuracy — and record the results to improve it.
* [Self-Organized Learning Groups](self-organized_learning_groups.md) - Learning cohorts formed by employees to learn from each other on topics like improving Scrum processes or new skills such as technical tools or programming languages.
* [Self-Regulated Strategy Development (SRSD)](self-regulated_strategy_development_(srsd).md) - SRSD is an instructional approach designed to enhance students' writing skills by addressing their affective, behavioral, and cognitive characteristics.
* [Self-Regulation - Mindfulness Techniques](self-regulation_-_mindfulness_techniques.md) - Incorporate mindfulness techniques to help learners manage their initial emotional impulses and reactivity.
* [Self-Regulation - Scaffolding](self-regulation_-_scaffolding.md) - Scaffolding self-regulation means providing temporary external structure — prompts, routines, and co-regulation from a facilitator — so learners can practice managing emotional and behavioral responses in an emotionally safe context.
* [Self-Regulation Scaffolding](self-regulation_scaffolding.md) - Scaffolding provides learners with a safe and emotionally supportive context to learn how to manage their responses to challenging or stimulating situations.
* [Self-Reinforcement](self-reinforcement.md) - Individuals select a reinforcer and reward themselves for reaching or exceeding a set performance criterion.
* [Self-Reinforcement in Writing](self-reinforcement_in_writing.md) - Self-reinforcement involves students delivering effort-based, behavior-specific praise and rewards to themselves during the writing process, building self-regulation and persistence.
* [Self-Talk Brainstorm](self-talk_brainstorm.md) - Provide learners with scenarios of challenging situations and have them brainstorm possible neutral or positive self-talk responses.
* [Semantic Feature Analysis](semantic_feature_analysis.md) - Learners analyze a set of related concepts against a matrix of features, marking presence or absence of each feature to build relational vocabulary knowledge.
* [Semantic Gradients And Context Clues](semantic-gradients-and-context-clues.md) - A vocabulary strategy pairing ordered word continua (semantic gradients) with explicit instruction in inferring word meanings from surrounding text context.
* [Sensitivity to Language Concerns](sensitivity_to_language_concerns.md) - Celebrating linguistic diversity and tailoring instructional materials and discourse to multilingual learners so that language proficiency does not become a barrier to content learning.
* [Sensory Breaks](sensory-breaks.md) - Short, structured pauses involving movement, sensory input, or mindfulness that restore attention and regulate arousal during extended learning sessions.
* [Sensory Processing and Handwriting](sensory_processing_and_handwriting.md) - Activities that incorporate sensory experiences (tactile, proprioceptive, visual) to support handwriting skill development.
* [Sensory Stimulation](sensory_stimulation.md) - Sensory stimulation involves providing experiences that engage a person's senses to improve regulation, attention, and focus.
* [Sensory Tools and Fidgets](sensory_tools_and_fidgets.md) - Small manipulable objects or sensory supports provided to learners to channel restlessness, support self-regulation, and sustain attention during learning tasks.
* [Sentence Combining](sentence-combining.md) - Learners take short, kernel sentences and combine them into more complex, mature sentences using coordination, subordination, and embedding.
* [Sentence Combining](sentence_combining.md) - Practice how to combine two simple sentences into a compound or complex sentence.
* [Sentence Expansion](sentence-expansion.md) - Learners elaborate a short base sentence into richer, more complex sentences using targeted prompts (who, what, when, where, why, how), building syntactic control and content knowledge simultaneously.
* [Sentence Frames](sentence-frames.md) - Sentence frames provide partially completed sentence starters that structure learners' oral or written responses, supporting academic language production and respectful dialogue.
* [Sentence Frames](sentence_frames.md) - Provide partially completed sentence starters that structure academic or dialogic language so learners can participate in discussion and writing beyond their current independent production level.
* [Sentence Frames And Starters](sentence-frames-and-starters.md) - Partially completed sentence templates that give learners the linguistic structure to express academic ideas, reducing language demands so cognitive effort goes to content.
* [Sentence Starters](sentence_starters.md) - Every student writes a sentence in response to a prompt before whole-class sharing, replacing hand-raising with universal written participation.
* [Sentence Stems for Reflection](sentence_stems_for_reflection.md) - Teachers can use sentence stems to stimulate reflection and make it more accessible to learners.
* [Separate Product, Process, and Progress Criteria](separate_product,_process,_and_progress_criteria.md) - Instead of blending behavior and participation into one grade, differentiate between grades for product (achievement), process (learning behaviors), and progress (growth over time).
* [Separating Effort From Achievement](separating-effort-from-achievement.md) - Designing feedback, grading, and classroom discourse so that effort and progress are recognized independently of performance outcomes, protecting motivation while keeping achievement standards intact.
* [Service Learning](service-learning.md) - A teaching strategy that integrates meaningful community service with academic instruction and structured reflection, so learners apply course concepts to authentic community-identified needs.
* [Service Learning](service_learning.md) - A teaching strategy that integrates meaningful community service with academic instruction and structured reflection, so learners apply course content to authentic community-identified needs.
* [Set a High Bar for Each Student](set_a_high_bar_for_each_student.md) - Hold high expectations for all students and convince them they can succeed through social-psychological interventions that build self-efficacy and a learner mindset.
* [Set Aside Your Own Reaction](set_aside_your_own_reaction.md) - Responding with empathy means letting students' reactions come first.
* [Set Clear Objectives](set_clear_objectives.md) - Objectives aligned with standards, activities, and assessments, providing students with a clear understanding of the knowledge and skills they will demonstrate.
* [Setting Achievable Micro-Goals](setting_achievable_micro-goals.md) - Breaking larger learning objectives into small, immediately achievable steps so that repeated success builds momentum, self-efficacy, and sustained engagement.
* [Setting Goals](setting_goals.md) - Lessons have clear learning intentions with goals that clarify what success looks like.
* [Seven Blind Mice](seven_blind_mice.md) - Learners hear the story 'Seven Blind Mice,' stopping after each mouse's perspective to take notes or engage in jigsaw reading, then synthesize the partial viewpoints into a whole.
* [Shadow a Student](shadow_a_student.md) - Shadowing involves following a student throughout their school day to gain insights into their experiences, challenges, and needs.
* [Shadowing (Behavior Adjustment)](shadowing_(behavior_adjustment).md) - An adult follows a child closely, within arm's reach, to provide constant direct supervision and prevent unwanted behaviors (e.g., biting) before they occur.
* [Shadowing (for behavior adjustment)](shadowing_(for_behavior_adjustment).md) - An adult follows a child everywhere they go, staying within arm's reach to prevent unwanted behavior before it happens.
* [Shadowing a Student](shadowing_a_student.md) - Shadowing is a direct observation technique where an adult follows a student throughout their school day to gain real-time qualitative evidence of the student's experiences.
* [Shadowing for Behavior Adjustment](shadowing_for_behavior_adjustment.md) - An adult maintains close, continuous proximity to a child to prevent harmful behaviors before they occur, then coaches replacement behaviors.
* [Shadowing to Address Behavior Problems](shadowing_to_address_behavior_problems.md) - An adult shadow provides close, continuous, low-key supervision of a student exhibiting harmful behavior to prevent incidents and coach replacement behaviors in real time.
* [Shadowing to Adjust Behavior Problems](shadowing_to_adjust_behavior_problems.md) - An adult stays within arm's reach of a child who exhibits harmful behaviors (e.g., biting, hitting) to prevent incidents and coach replacement behaviors in the moment.
* [Shape Search](shape_search.md) - Learners identify two- and three-dimensional shapes in their surroundings, promoting geometric awareness and real-world application of geometric concepts.
* [Share Teacher's Written Work](share_teacher's_written_work.md) - Instructors share their own written work with learners, soliciting feedback and criticism to model the value of continuous improvement.
* [Shared Reading](shared-reading.md) - An interactive read-aloud in which an adult models fluent reading while learners follow the text, participate through prediction and questioning, and gradually take over parts of the reading.
* [Shared Reading](shared_reading.md) - An interactive read-aloud in which an adult models fluent reading while learners follow the text, participate in prediction and discussion, and gradually take over parts of the reading.
* [Shared Reflection](shared_reflection.md) - A classroom practice in which students publicly share reflections on their academic work—both successes and struggles—so that the class collectively supports each member's ongoing improvement.
* [Shared Writing](shared-writing.md) - Teacher and students collaboratively compose a text, with the teacher scribing student-generated ideas and guiding revision.
* [Shared Writing](shared_writing.md) - Shared writing involves a teacher and students collaboratively composing a text, with the teacher acting as a scribe who records and refines student-generated ideas.
* [Sharing and Discussion of Independent Reading](sharing_and_discussion_of_independent_reading.md) - After independent reading, gather students to talk about their books through structured activities such as book clubs, peer recommendations, or creating commercials to interest others.
* [Shoeless Learning Spaces](shoeless_learning_spaces.md) - Learners remove their shoes in the learning space, creating a home-like, relaxed environment intended to lower stress and increase comfort and engagement.
* [Short Conversations (Pre-Reading)](short_conversations_(pre-reading).md) - Learners discuss a single sentence from an upcoming reading in pairs, constrained to a maximum of three words per speaking turn.
* [Show and Tell for Multimodal Composition](show_and_tell_for_multimodal_composition.md) - Brief, recurring sessions where learners show multimodal design choices or narrate their composition process, functioning as an author's chair for multimodal work.
* [Show and Tell for Multimodal Design](show_and_tell_for_multimodal_design.md) - Devoting time each week for students to show the effects of different multimodal combinations or tell about their multimodal composition process.
* [Show Me](show_me.md) - Use slides or the board to provide a visual, permanent channel for information or directions that are also delivered verbally.
* [Sift Method](sift-method.md) - A four-move strategy (Stop, Investigate the source, Find better coverage, Trace claims) for evaluating online information quickly through lateral rather than vertical reading.
* [SIFT Method](sift_method.md) - A four-move strategy (Stop, Investigate the source, Find better coverage, Trace claims) for efficiently evaluating the credibility of online information.
* [Signage and Displays](signage_and_displays.md) - Deliberate design of wall displays, signage, and visual artifacts in the learning environment to support orientation, belonging, and identity — without adding visual clutter that competes for attention.
* [Signaling](signaling.md) - Adding non-content cues (highlights, arrows, color, headings, vocal emphasis) that direct attention to essential material without changing the content itself.
* [Signaling and Highlighting](signaling_and_highlighting.md) - Directing learner attention to essential content through visual, verbal, or typographic cues so that limited attention is allocated to what matters most.
* [Silent Countdown](silent_countdown.md) - A non-verbal attention signal in which the teacher silently counts down (e.g., with raised fingers) to give students a predictable window to transition to quiet attention.
* [Silent Game Incentive](silent_game_incentive.md) - A gamified classroom-management routine in which students earn collective game-board advancement by meeting a timed quietness goal after an activity.
* [Simple-to-Difficult Examples](simple-to-difficult_examples.md) - Start with simple examples when introducing a concept, then progress to more complex ones later.
* [Simplify and Clarify Instructions](simplify_and_clarify_instructions.md) - Ensure formulations are short, easy to understand, and precise.
* [Simplify Grading Scales](simplify_grading_scales.md) - Replace fine-grained percentage scales (0–100) with coarse scales (e.g., 0–4) so that grades describe performance levels rather than accumulating arithmetic penalties.
* [Simplifying Language](simplifying-language.md) - Rewriting instructional text and speech into shorter sentences, plainer vocabulary, and clearer structure to reduce linguistic processing demands.
* [Simulation Based Learning](simulation-based-learning.md) - Learners practice skills in an interactive, modeled environment that approximates a real task, receiving feedback on their performance without real-world stakes.
* [Simulation Based Medical Training](simulation-based-medical-training.md) - Learners rehearse clinical skills and decisions in realistic but risk-free simulated environments — manikins, task trainers, standardized patients, or virtual scenarios — followed by structured debriefing.
* [Simulation Based Practice](simulation-based-practice.md) - Learners rehearse a task in a simplified, safe, interactive replica of a real-world environment, receiving feedback on performance without real-world consequences.
* [Simulation Based Training](simulation-based-training.md) - Learners practice skills in a replicated or synthetic task environment where errors are safe and performance can be systematically varied, observed, and debriefed.
* [Simulation Learning](simulation_learning.md) - Simulation learning engages learners in active, role-based experiences that model simplified versions of reality, allowing them to act, see consequences, and learn from each other without real-world risk.
* [Simulations](simulations.md) - Simulations use electronic or software-based activities to simulate a real-world situation to which a learner must react, allowing safe practice and consequence exploration.
* [Simulations + Structured Activity](simulations_+_structured_activity.md) - Students engage with simulations designed to provide hands-on experience with real-world scenarios or abstract concepts, paired with a structured activity that directs attention, prompts decisions, and consolidates learning.
* [Simultaneous Multisensory Instruction](simultaneous_multisensory_instruction.md) - Teaching language skills by having learners simultaneously see, say, hear, and write/trace the same content, linking multiple sensory modalities to one linguistic unit.
* [Singing](singing.md) - Using sung melody and rhythm to encode, rehearse, and retrieve verbal content or to build musical/vocal skill itself.
* [Six Consumer Questions](six_consumer_questions.md) - A set of six questions designed to help evaluate the trustworthiness of a news story.
* [Six Thinking Hats](six_thinking_hats.md) - A parallel thinking process that separates thinking into six functions — each symbolized by a colored 'hat' — so groups can focus on one mode of thinking at a time and switch deliberately between them.
* [Skimming](skimming.md) - Skimming involves reviewing a text quickly, focusing on headings, captions, and highlighted information, to gain familiarity with the material before reading it thoroughly.
* [Skimming And Scanning](skimming-and-scanning.md) - Rapid reading techniques for locating gist or specific information in a text without reading every word.
* [Skip Counting with Movement](skip_counting_with_movement.md) - Make up a movement that comes in 2, 3, or more parts.
* [Slug](slug.md) - A slug is a short, memorable verbal label learners attach to an idea, case, or step to compress it into a retrievable cue.
* [Small Group Instruction](small_group_instruction.md) - Small group instruction involves a teacher working with a small number of students on a specific skill or concept, enabling targeted support, immediate feedback, and tailored scaffolding.
* [Small Group Phonemic Awareness Intervention](small-group-phonemic-awareness-intervention.md) - Targeted small-group instruction that develops learners' ability to hear, identify, and manipulate the individual sounds (phonemes) in spoken words.
* [SMART Goal Setting](smart_goal_setting.md) - SMART goal setting ensures goals are Specific, Measurable, Attainable, Relevant, and Time-Specific.
* [Snap It](snap_it.md) - A game where children make a train of connecting cubes, snap the train into two parts, hide one part behind their back, and show the remaining cubes.
* [Snowball Discussion](snowball_discussion.md) - Learners begin in pairs, responding to a discussion question only with a single partner.
* [SOAP (Specific, Observable, Appropriate, Personal)](soap_(specific,_observable,_appropriate,_personal).md) - SOAP is an acronym that guides teachers in selecting behaviors for students to self-monitor, ensuring each target behavior is well-defined and suitable for the individual learner.
* [SOAR Feedback Method](soar_feedback_method.md) - The SOAR method maximizes the impact of feedback on student writing by ensuring it is Specific, Ongoing, Action-Oriented, and Reasonable.
* [Social Annotation](social_annotation.md) - Social annotation is a collaborative method where learners add notes, comments, and multimedia to shared texts and media, turning reading into an anchored, interactive conversation.
* [Social Awareness - Model Empathy and Explain](social_awareness_-_model_empathy_and_explain.md) - The facilitator models empathic reasoning aloud and leads explicit discussions of perspectives, emotions, and social issues — including social justice topics — using current events and fictional characters as anchors.
* [Social Awareness Instruction with Current Events](social_awareness_instruction_with_current_events.md) - Using current events and fictional characters to explicitly teach social awareness, perspective-taking, and empathy through structured discussion.
* [Social Emotional Learning Curricula](social-emotional-learning-curricula.md) - Structured, sequenced programs that explicitly teach self-awareness, self-management, social awareness, relationship skills, and responsible decision-making.
* [Social Emotional Learning Integration](social-emotional-learning-integration.md) - Embedding social-emotional competencies (self-awareness, self-management, social awareness, relationship skills, responsible decision-making) into academic instruction rather than teaching them as a separate program.
* [Social Emotional Learning Routines](social-emotional-learning-routines.md) - Recurring, structured classroom practices that build students' self-awareness, self-management, social awareness, relationship skills, and responsible decision-making.
* [Social Norms Marketing](social-norms-marketing.md) - Correcting learners' misperceptions of peer behavior by publicizing accurate normative data, so that perceived social pressure shifts toward healthier or more productive behavior.
* [Social Norms Marketing Campaign](social-norms-marketing-campaign.md) - A campaign strategy that corrects learners' misperceptions of peer behavior and attitudes by publicizing accurate normative data, reducing pressure to conform to exaggerated norms.
* [Socratic Questioning](socratic_questioning.md) - A strategy in which the instructor teaches through a disciplined sequence of probing questions, guiding learners to articulate, examine, and revise their own reasoning rather than receiving explanations directly.
* [Socratic Seminar](socratic-seminar.md) - A structured, dialogue-based discussion in which participants question, analyze, and build meaning from a shared text through open-ended inquiry rather than teacher-led explanation.
* [Socratic Seminar](socratic_seminar.md) - A structured, open-ended dialogue in which learners question a shared text or problem, building understanding through reasoned discussion rather than teacher explanation.
* [Socratic Seminars](socratic_seminars.md) - A text-based, facilitator-guided discussion format in which students question, listen, and build meaning collaboratively through open-ended dialogue.
* [Soliciting Feedback on Instruction](soliciting_feedback_on_instruction.md) - Instructors systematically gather, interpret, and act on learner feedback about their teaching to improve instructional quality.
* [Solve Before You Teach](solve-before-you-teach.md) - The instructor works through the task themselves before teaching it, surfacing solution paths, misconceptions, and decision points so instruction anticipates rather than reacts to learner difficulty.
* [Songs and Rhymes](songs_and_rhymes.md) - Setting information to melody, rhythm, or rhyme to leverage prosodic structure as a retrieval scaffold for verbal recall.
* [Songwriting](songwriting.md) - Students create their own verses or entire songs to build phonemic awareness, vocabulary, and language development through creative, multisensory composition.
* [Sort And Classify](sort-and-classify.md) - Learners group items into categories based on shared features, inducing or applying classification rules through active comparison.
* [Sorting and Classifying](sorting_and_classifying.md) - Learners group items into categories based on shared features, either into given categories or ones they construct themselves.
* [Sound Walls](sound_walls.md) - A classroom visual reference organized by phonemes (not letters) that supports explicit teaching of letter-sound correspondence, phonemic awareness, and phonics.
* [Sound-blocking Devices](sound-blocking_devices.md) - Sound-blocking devices, such as noise-reducing headphones or earmuffs, can help children with auditory sensitivities manage sensory input by reducing background noise.
* [Source Evaluation](source-evaluation.md) - Teaching learners to judge the credibility, authority, and reliability of information sources before using them.
* [Sourcing](sourcing.md) - Sourcing teaches learners to ask who created a document, when, why, and under what circumstances before using its content — treating the origin of information as evidence about its reliability.
* [Spaced Learning Through Activity Breaks](spaced_learning_through_activity_breaks.md) - Breaking up learning or work into short chunks of time interspersed with activity breaks leverages the 'spacing effect,' a well-replicated phenomenon in psychology.
* [Spaced Practice](spaced_practice.md) - Spaced practice distributes learning sessions and reviews over time, contrasting with massed practice (cramming), to improve long-term retention.
* [Spaced Practice Scheduling](spaced-practice-scheduling.md) - Distributing practice sessions over time rather than massing them together, exploiting the spacing effect to improve long-term retention.
* [Spaced Repetition](spaced_repetition.md) - Distributing review of material across multiple sessions separated by increasing time intervals, rather than massing it into one session.
* [Spaced Repetition Scheduling](spaced-repetition-scheduling.md) - Scheduling review of material at increasing intervals over time rather than massing it into a single session, so each review occurs just as forgetting begins.
* [Spaced Retrieval](spaced-retrieval.md) - Scheduling recall attempts at increasing intervals over time so that effortful retrieval, rather than rereading, drives durable memory.
* [Spaced Retrieval Practice](spaced-retrieval-practice.md) - Scheduling recall attempts across increasing intervals of time so that learners must reconstruct knowledge from memory rather than re-expose themselves to it.
* [Spaced Retrieval Practice](spaced_retrieval_practice.md) - Distributing retrieval attempts over time and across sessions so learners must reconstruct knowledge from memory rather than re-expose themselves to it.
* [Spaced Scheduling](spaced-scheduling.md) - Distributing learning episodes and reviews over time rather than massing them together, exploiting the spacing effect to improve long-term retention.
* [Spaces that Foster Creativity](spaces_that_foster_creativity.md) - Deliberately designing the physical (and virtual) learning environment — flexible furniture, varied activity zones, learner work on display, and natural light — to support creative work, collaboration, and engagement.
* [Spacing Activities in Handwriting](spacing_activities_in_handwriting.md) - Activities designed to improve spacing between letters and words, which is crucial for overall legibility.
* [Spark Engagement with Student-Generated Questions](spark_engagement_with_student-generated_questions.md) - Students formulate questions and investigate topics they are curious about or consider important.
* [Speak the Future, Design the Future (Changing the Discourse)](speak_the_future,_design_the_future_(changing_the_discourse).md) - A design strategy that treats language as a lever for equity — critiquing deficit-oriented "Discourse I" language and deliberately replacing it with transformative "Discourse II" language during school design work.
* [Specific Feedback](specific_feedback.md) - Providing targeted feedback tied directly to specific words, phrases, sentences, or paragraphs in student writing, addressing both content and craft.
* [Specific Goal Questioning](specific_goal_questioning.md) - Design questions with a specific goal in mind, such as identifying grabbers or understanding essay structure.
* [Specific Technique Identification](specific_technique_identification.md) - Providing specific guidance on what to look for in a text, naming sentences or words whenever possible, helps students learn from the text.
* [Speech-to-Text Technology](speech-to-text_technology.md) - Speech-to-text (dictation) technology converts spoken language into written text, allowing learners to compose by voice rather than by spelling, handwriting, or typing.
* [Speed Chatting (Pre-Reading)](speed_chatting_(pre-reading).md) - A pre-reading activity in which learners rotate through brief timed pair conversations about a topic, activating prior knowledge and building engagement before reading.
* [Spelling Instruction](spelling-instruction.md) - Systematic teaching of spelling through explicit pattern instruction, orthographic mapping, and distributed practice rather than rote memorization of word lists.
* [Spelling Instruction Through Encoding](spelling_instruction_through_encoding.md) - Teaching spelling by having learners write words from dictation and construct spellings from sound-letter knowledge, rather than only reading or copying them.
* [Spelling Retrieval Practice](spelling-retrieval-practice.md) - Learners practice spelling by generating word spellings from memory rather than copying or passively re-reading them.
* [Spelling Retrieval Practice](spelling_retrieval_practice.md) - Learners practice spelling by actively retrieving and producing word spellings from memory rather than re-reading or copying them.
* [SQ3R](sq3r.md) - A five-step reading strategy — Survey, Question, Read, Recite, Review — that structures text study around previewing, goal-setting, and self-testing.
* [Squeeze Ball or Theraputty](squeeze_ball_or_theraputty.md) - Providing a squeeze ball or Theraputty offers a tactile outlet for students to manage frustration and agitation.
* [SRSD (Self-Regulated Strategy Development)](srsd-self-regulated-strategy-development.md) - An instructional approach that explicitly teaches writing (and other) strategies alongside the self-regulation skills — goal setting, self-monitoring, self-instruction, and self-reinforcement — needed to deploy them independently.
* [Stages of Drawing and Writing Development](stages_of_drawing_and_writing_development.md) - Children progress through predictable stages of drawing and writing development — from random scribbling to controlled scribbling, lines and patterns, pictures of objects or people, and letter and word practice — as fine motor control and symbolic understanding mature.
* [Stand Up Sit Down](stand-up-sit-down.md) - A whole-body response game in which students stand or sit based on the (often disguised) value of a mentally computed number, turning fact retrieval into fast-paced kinesthetic practice.
* [Standard Inverted Classroom](standard_inverted_classroom.md) - Learners study lecture videos and other materials before class, freeing class time for practice, application, and individualized support.
* [Standard-Based Regrouping](standard-based_regrouping.md) - Form homogeneous groups based on learners' mastery of specific standards.
* [Standards-/Mastery-Based Grading](standards-mastery-based-grading.md) - Grades are assigned based on demonstrated mastery of explicitly stated learning objectives rather than accumulated points, averages, or compliance behaviors.
* [Standards-Based Grading](standards-based-grading.md) - Shift from arbitrary points and percentages to evaluating students on mastery of specific learning standards, making expectations clear and grades meaningful.
* [Standards-Based Grading](standards-based_grading.md) - Shift from arbitrary points and percentages to evaluating students on mastery of specific learning standards, making expectations clear and grades meaningful.
* [Start with Yourself (Identity Mapping)](start_with_yourself_(identity_mapping).md) - Educators reflect on their own intersectional identities and how those identities shape their perceptions, biases, and relationships with students before attempting empathy- or equity-oriented work.
* [Station Rotation](station-rotation.md) - Learners move between fixed stations — teacher-led, collaborative, and independent/digital — on a set schedule, so one teacher can differentiate instruction within a single class period.
* [Station Rotation](station_rotation.md) - Students rotate through a fixed sequence of learning stations — typically including teacher-led, collaborative, and digital/independent work — within a single lesson or class period.
* [Stations: Interacting with Multiple Texts](stations-interacting_with_multiple_texts.md) - Small groups rotate through stations, each featuring a different text or media type, to build multi-perspective understanding of a topic.
* [Steelmanning](steelmanning.md) - Learners construct the strongest possible version of a position they disagree with before critiquing it, deepening understanding and improving argument quality.
* [Step In When Abusive Language Occurs](step_in_when_abusive_language_occurs.md) - Addressing offensive language promptly and consistently is crucial for creating a safe and respectful classroom environment.
* [Stick-It Together (Collaborative Problem-Solving)](stick-it_together_(collaborative_problem-solving).md) - Students solve a problem individually on sticky notes, then merge their ideas into a single group answer, defending and refining each other's thinking.
* [Stop and Jot](stop-and-jot.md) - A processing activity in which learners pause at designated points in a lesson to write brief responses to instructor-posed questions before instruction continues.
* [Stop and Jot](stop_and_jot.md) - A processing activity that provides structured opportunities for learners to pause and respond in writing to key questions during a lesson.
* [Storage Areas](storage_areas.md) - Deliberately organized, well-labeled storage of materials and resources in a learning space so that learners can find, access, and return materials with minimal distraction and disruption.
* [Story and Concept Maps](story_and_concept_maps.md) - Story and concept maps are visual representations used to organize information and enhance comprehension, particularly of narratives and complex relationships.
* [Story Map](story_map.md) - Story maps are graphic organizers that help learners visually represent the key elements of a narrative — characters, setting, plot, conflict, and resolution — to support comprehension and recall.
* [Story Mapping](story-mapping.md) - Learners construct a visual map of a narrative's key story grammar elements — characters, setting, problem, events, and resolution — to improve comprehension and recall.
* [Story Retelling](story-retelling.md) - Learners reconstruct a narrative they have read or heard in their own words, demonstrating comprehension of sequence, characters, and causal structure.
* [Story Telling (Pre-Reading)](story_telling_(pre-reading).md) - The instructor prepares and tells a short, personalized narrative related to the reading topic to build interest and activate prior knowledge before reading.
* [Storybook Emotion Analysis](storybook_emotion_analysis.md) - Using storybooks to explore and discuss the emotions of characters, building emotional recognition, empathy, and perspective-taking.
* [Storytelling](storytelling.md) - Embedding learning content in a narrative structure — characters, causality, and sequence — to leverage the mind's affinity for stories in encoding, organization, and recall.
* [Storytelling in Explainer Animations and Videos](storytelling_in_explainer_animations_and_videos.md) - Combining visual elements with engaging narratives to capture the learners' attention and facilitate long-term information retention.
* [Strategic Desk Arrangement](strategic_desk_arrangement.md) - Deliberately configuring the physical seating layout to align with the intended learning activity, attention demands, and learner needs.
* [Strategies to Develop Positive Classroom Pride](strategies_to_develop_positive_classroom_pride.md) - A cluster of practices — displaying learner work, regular verbal reinforcement, public recognition, and extending recognition to families — that builds collective pride and a positive classroom climate.
* [Strategies to Remember Name Pronunciation](strategies_to_remember_name_pronunciation.md) - Use mnemonic and technological techniques — word association, phonetic respelling, rhyming, and name-pronunciation tools — to learn and correctly pronounce learners' names.
* [Strategy Identification & Reflection](strategy_identification_&_reflection.md) - Learners name and reflect on the strategies they use to overcome challenges, building a shared, reusable vocabulary of learning processes.
* [Strategy Identification and Application](strategy_identification_and_application.md) - Explicitly teaching a repertoire of problem-solving strategies, then having learners name and reflect on the strategies they use when stuck, so they can reapply them independently.
* [Strategy Instruction](strategy_instruction.md) - Strategy instruction teaches learners how to learn effectively by applying principles, rules, or multi-step processes to solve problems or accomplish learning tasks.
* [Strategy Instruction (Self-Regulated Strategy Development - SRSD)](strategy_instruction_(self-regulated_strategy_development_-_srsd).md) - Explicit teaching of writing strategies, especially self-regulated strategy development (SRSD).
* [Strength-Based Approach to Responsible Decision-Making](strength-based_approach_to_responsible_decision-making.md) - Identifying and leveraging a student's existing strengths to build competence in responsible decision-making.
* [Strength-Based Approach to Self-Awareness](strength-based_approach_to_self-awareness.md) - Identifies a student's existing strengths in self-awareness (emotion labeling, reflection, prediction) and leverages them to address related challenges.
* [Strength-Based Approach to Social Awareness](strength-based_approach_to_social_awareness.md) - This method involves identifying a student's strengths related to social awareness and using those strengths to address their challenges.
* [Strength-Based Strategies for Responsible Decision-Making](strength-based_strategies_for_responsible_decision-making.md) - Educators identify students' strengths related to responsible decision-making and leverage these strengths to address challenges.
* [Strength-Based Strategies for Self-Awareness](strength-based_strategies_for_self-awareness.md) - Educators identify and support students by recognizing their strengths related to self-awareness and using these strengths to address challenges.
* [Strength-Based Strategies for Social Awareness](strength-based_strategies_for_social_awareness.md) - Educators identify students' existing social-emotional strengths and deliberately leverage them to build social awareness and address interpersonal challenges.
* [Strengths Based Feedback](strengths-based-feedback.md) - Feedback that identifies and builds on what learners are already doing well, using those strengths as the entry point for improvement rather than leading with deficits.
* [Strengths-Based IEPs](strengths-based_ieps.md) - A shift in mindset where the Individualized Education Program (IEP) focuses as much on the learner's strengths as it does on their weaknesses, leveraging abilities to help address needs.
* [Strengths-Based Teaching](strengths-based_teaching.md) - A teaching approach that identifies and leverages learners' existing strengths and talents to build engagement, confidence, and overall achievement rather than remediating deficits.
* [Stress Management Routines](stress-management-routines.md) - Regularly embedded practices — breathing, mindfulness, physical movement, and cognitive reframing — that help learners regulate stress so it does not consume working memory and attention needed for learning.
* [Structured Academic Controversy](structured-academic-controversy.md) - A cooperative learning procedure in which pairs of students research and advocate opposing positions on an issue, then reverse perspectives, reach consensus, and report jointly.
* [Structured Academic Controversy](structured_academic_controversy.md) - A cooperative learning procedure in which pairs of opposing teams research, advocate, and then reverse perspectives on a contested issue.
* [Structured and Sustained Family Engagement Programs](structured_and_sustained_family_engagement_programs.md) - School-wide programs that embed family participation in students' academic learning, sustained across years and evaluated for effectiveness.
* [Structured Discussion Techniques](structured_discussion_techniques.md) - Facilitating productive discussions through clear expectations, modeling, sentence starters, and repeatable talk routines so learners can sustain rigorous peer dialogue with minimal instructor supervision.
* [Structured Music and Dance Activities](structured_music_and_dance_activities.md) - Structured music and dance activities embed rhythm and movement into educational content, integrating academic, motor, and social goals — particularly for young learners.
