* [Structured Observation Protocol](structured_observation_protocol.md) - A structured observation protocol uses a template or guide to record factual details about a student's experiences without interpretation, opinion, or judgment.
* [Structured Peer Discussion Roles](structured-peer-discussion-roles.md) - Assigning learners distinct, interdependent roles (e.g., questioner, summarizer, skeptic) within small-group discussion to ensure equitable participation and deeper processing.
* [Structured Questioning for Pair Talk](structured_questioning_for_pair_talk.md) - Using structured questions with clear goals and timeframes provides focus and scaffolding for pair talk activities.
* [Structured/Segmented Instruction](structured-segmented-instruction.md) - Systematically teaching information that has been chunked into manageable pieces and sequenced so parts are mastered before being integrated into the whole.
* [Structuring Group Work](structuring-group-work.md) - Designing collaborative tasks with defined roles, individual accountability, and explicit interaction protocols so group activity produces learning rather than mere activity.
* [Structuring Lessons](structuring_lessons.md) - A lesson structure maps the teaching and learning that occurs in class.
* [Student Assessment Reports](student_assessment_reports.md) - Student assessment reports are structured, often visual documents that communicate learner progress and assessment results to students, parents, and other stakeholders.
* [Student Blogging](student_blogging.md) - Students regularly publish written posts to an authentic audience, often with peer commenting, to support reflection, writing development, and learner autonomy.
* [Student Choice](student_choice.md) - Allowing learners to make meaningful decisions about their learning — what to work on, how to approach it, or how to demonstrate mastery — to build autonomy, motivation, and self-direction.
* [Student Created Magazines](student-created_magazines.md) - Learners research, write, edit, and design a magazine issue for a real audience, combining disciplinary writing with authentic publication.
* [Student Demonstrations](student_demonstrations.md) - Learners showcase their understanding of a topic or skill through a performance, presentation, or hands-on activity, typically as part of assessment.
* [Student Feedback on the Class](student_feedback_on_the_class.md) - Systematically soliciting student feedback about instruction and using it to adjust teaching practice, course design, and classroom climate.
* [Student Game Design](student_game_design.md) - Learners build their own game around a course topic or principle, designing mechanics, rules, and content that teach the material to an intended audience.
* [Student Hand Signals](student_hand_signals.md) - Learners use pre-defined non-verbal hand cues to simultaneously indicate understanding, agreement, or readiness in response to a prompt.
* [Student Led Conferences](student-led-conferences.md) - A conference format in which students prepare and deliver a presentation of their own learning, goals, and work samples to teachers and families, replacing the teacher-dominated report.
* [Student Led IEPs](student-led-ieps.md) - Students with disabilities actively lead their Individualized Education Program (IEP) meetings — presenting goals, reporting progress, and advocating for accommodations — rather than passively attending them.
* [Student Led Math Conferences](student_led_math_conferences.md) - One-on-one conferences in which students bring their own work and formative assessment data, reflect on their mathematical progress, and co-construct a specific learning goal with the teacher.
* [Student Models](student_models.md) - Student models are examples of writing (or other work) produced by other students, typically curated and anonymized by instructors, made available for learners to study as reference points.
* [Student Portfolio Creation](student_portfolio_creation.md) - Student portfolio creation involves students selecting and organizing their best work samples to demonstrate their progress, reflect on their learning, and set goals.
* [Student Presentation of Collaborative Work](student_presentation_of_collaborative_work.md) - After completing a collaborative problem-solving task, groups project their work and present their final responses to the class, explaining their reasoning and process.
* [Student Presentations All About Me](student_presentations-all_about_me.md) - Learners prepare and deliver a short personal presentation ("All About Me") to build oral communication skills, classroom community, and self-knowledge.
* [Student Reflection (Responsible Decision-Making)](student_reflection_(responsible_decision-making).md) - Uses structured student reflection on real and hypothetical decisions to build the CASEL competency of responsible decision-making — evaluating consequences, ethical standards, and social norms before acting.
* [Student Self-Knowledge](student_self-knowledge.md) - Student self-knowledge involves helping learners understand their strengths, interests, and areas for growth as learners.
* [Student Shadowing](student_shadowing.md) - An adult (educator, administrator, or school leader) follows a student throughout their school day to observe their experiences, interactions, and environment firsthand.
* [Student Shadowing (For Educator Insights)](student_shadowing_(for_educator_insights).md) - Educators spend a full or substantial portion of a school day shadowing an individual student to gather real-time qualitative data about the learner's lived experience.
* [Student Shadowing (for high school instructors)](student_shadowing_(for_high_school_instructors).md) - A veteran teacher or coach shadows one or two students for a couple of days to gain insights into their experiences.
* [Student Shadowing (Qualitative Data Collection)](student_shadowing_(qualitative_data_collection).md) - A school leader spends a full school day shadowing an individual student to gather qualitative data about their lived experience of school.
* [Student Shadowing (Teacher/Coach Observation)](student-shadowing-teacher-coach-observation.md) - A teacher or coach shadows a student to gain insights into their experiences and identify areas for improvement in teaching practices.
* [Student Voice In Curriculum Planning](student-voice-in-curriculum-planning.md) - Involving learners directly in decisions about what and how the curriculum covers — from topic selection and pacing to assessment design — rather than treating them as passive recipients of a fixed plan.
* [Student-Created Magazines](student-created-magazines.md) - Students research real-world applications of a subject area, read primary sources closely, and write and assemble articles into a collaborative magazine.
* [Student-Driven Classroom Transformation](student-driven_classroom_transformation.md) - Involving students in the design and transformation of their learning environment to promote ownership, engagement, and a sense of community.
* [Student-Generated Problems](student-generated_problems.md) - Learners create their own problems based on concepts they are learning, share them with peers, and solve each other's problems.
* [Student-Generated Questions](student-generated_questions.md) - Projects driven by student-generated questions allow learners to investigate topics they are curious about or think are important.
* [Student-Generated Questions in PBL](student-generated_questions_in_pbl.md) - Sparking engagement with student-generated questions involves encouraging students to investigate topics they care about and identify questions they want to answer.
* [Student-Led Conferences](student-led_conferences.md) - Meetings in which students take the lead in presenting and discussing their learning progress with families, with the teacher acting as facilitator rather than primary speaker.
* [Student-Pair Math Games](student-pair_math_games.md) - Pair-based games facilitate active learning through friendly competition, requiring students to recall and apply math skills in a dynamic setting.
* [Studio Critique](studio-critique.md) - A structured group feedback protocol in which learners present work-in-progress and receive facilitated peer and instructor critique to revise and improve it.
* [Study of Written Models](study_of_written_models.md) - Learners analyze and imitate well-crafted written models to internalize genre conventions, structures, and stylistic moves before producing their own writing.
* [Subitizing Activities](subitizing-activities.md) - Brief, repeated activities that build learners' ability to instantly recognize small quantities without counting, forming a foundation for arithmetic.
* [Success Criteria Co-Construction](success-criteria-co-construction.md) - Learners and teacher jointly develop the criteria that define quality in a task, before or during the work, so learners internalize what "good" looks like.
* [Summaries and Recaps](summaries_and_recaps.md) - Periodically restating key content in condensed, reworded, or re-represented form to consolidate understanding and support retention.
* [Summarization](summarization.md) - Explicit teaching of the elements of a summary of a text leads to improved ability and increased confidence in writing summaries.
* [Summarizing](summarizing.md) - Summarizing involves integrating important information from a text or problem to create a concise overview, including justifying solutions and explaining the strategies used.
* [Summative Assessment to Measure Learning Over Time](summative_assessment_to_measure_learning_over_time.md) - Interim and summative assessments measure learning over time — interims track individual progress toward standards, while summatives compare performance against standards at the end of an instructional period.
* [Supply Caddy](supply_caddy.md) - Consolidate learning supplies into a single portable, organized caddy so learners spend less time searching and more time learning.
* [Support Decoding of Text, Mathematical Notation, and Symbols](support_decoding_of_text,_mathematical_notation,_and_symbols.md) - Providing strategies and tools so that written text, mathematical notation, and other symbol systems do not become a barrier to the learning goal.
* [Support for Decoding Text, Mathematical Notation, and Symbols](support_for_decoding_text,_mathematical_notation,_and_symbols.md) - Ensuring that text and symbols do not impede the learning goal by providing necessary support for decoding.
* [Supported Independent Reading](supported_independent_reading.md) - Students read self-selected texts at their independent reading level during dedicated class time, with teacher guidance on book selection, conferring, and accountability structures.
* [Supporting Self-Regulation](supporting_self-regulation.md) - Identifying and supporting students who have learning strengths or challenges related to self-regulation.
* [Sushi Monster App](sushi_monster_app.md) - A free math app with a playful interface and characters to help kids expand their math skills and knowledge.
* [Sustained Silent Reading](sustained-silent-reading.md) - A scheduled period in which all members of a class or school read self-selected material silently, with the goal of building reading volume, fluency, and voluntary reading habits.
* [Syllabication Instruction](syllabication_instruction.md) - Teach children the six basic syllable types and how they affect spelling and decoding of multisyllable words.
* [Syllable Division Instruction](syllable-division-instruction.md) - Explicitly teaching learners to segment multisyllabic words into syllables using structural cues, so they can decode and spell long words independently.
* [Systematic Phonics Instruction](systematic-phonics-instruction.md) - Teaching letter–sound correspondences in a planned, sequential order with explicit instruction and practice, rather than incidentally or as needed.
* [Systematic Synthetic Phonics](systematic-synthetic-phonics.md) - Teaching letter–sound correspondences in a planned sequence, with learners blending graphemes to build and decode words from part to whole.

#### T {: #letter-t }

* [T-Chart](t-chart.md) - A T-chart is a graphic organizer shaped like the letter T, with a horizontal line at the top to signify a broad category and a vertical line that allows learners to compare attributes of a concept, organism, or phenomenon.
* [Tactile Toys / Fidgets](tactile-toys-fidgets.md) - Tactile toys and fidgets are self-regulation tools that provide movement and tactile input to support attention, focus, and sensory processing.
* [Tactile Toys for Sensory Integration](tactile_toys_for_sensory_integration.md) - Providing tactile toys (fidgets, textured objects, stress balls) to give learners sensory input that may support self-regulation, attention, and reduced fidgeting.
* [Take A Break Station](take_a_break_station.md) - A designated classroom space where students can decompress, reset, and practice self-regulation of emotions and behavior without stigma or consequence.
* [Take Action, Be Intentional](take_action,_be_intentional.md) - Deliberately shape classroom climate and teacher behavior so that every student's needs — academic, emotional, and material — are noticed and met.
* [Take Home Book Bags](take-home-book-bags.md) - A family literacy strategy in which curated bags of books and supporting materials are sent home with students to structure shared reading between children and caregivers.
* [Take Home_Practice_Sets](take-home_practice_sets.md) - Structured sets of practice problems or tasks assigned for completion outside class, extending retrieval and skill-building beyond instructional time.
* [Talk About Real-Life Challenges and Situations](talk_about_real-life_challenges_and_situations.md) - Facilitating discussions about the challenges learners are facing and how they feel about those situations.
* [Talk Moves](talk_moves.md) - Talk moves are sentence frames and conversational routines supplied to learners that help them express ideas, respond to peers, and interact in respectful, academically productive ways.
* [Talking to Young Children About Race and Racism](talking_to_young_children_about_race_and_racism.md) - Parents and caregivers initiate honest, developmentally appropriate conversations with young children about race, racism, and anti-racism, using media, books, and guided discussion.
* [Targeted and Specific Communication with Families](targeted_and_specific_communication_with_families.md) - Tailoring message content, language, and mode to specific family populations — such as non-native English speakers, families from different cultural backgrounds, and families of students in special education — so that communication is clear, relevant, and actionable.
* [Targeted Networking](targeted_networking.md) - Focusing relationship-building effort on specific people, groups, and activities aligned with one's goals and interests, prioritizing depth and shared purpose over breadth of contacts.
* [Task Analysis](task-analysis.md) - A systematic method for breaking down a complex skill or task into its component knowledge, skills, and decision points so instruction can be designed around what learners actually need to master.
* [Task Analysis](task_analysis.md) - Breaking a complex assignment into smaller, sequenced sub-tasks that learners can understand, sequence, and complete one at a time.
* [Task Force Teams of Inquiry](task_force_teams_of_inquiry.md) - Small teams form (or are formed) around self-selected topics from a brainstorm list, then research, investigate, and present their findings.
* [Task Management for Writing](task_management_for_writing.md) - Task management helps students plan, sequence, and monitor the work of a writing assignment — managing time, subtasks, and distractions — rather than treating writing as a single undifferentiated effort.
* [Teach Academic Vocabulary](teach_academic_vocabulary.md) - Provide explicit, planned instruction on the academic vocabulary learners will encounter in a unit or project, before and during that work.
* [Teach Key Words for Standardized Test Prompts](teach_key_words_for_standardized_test_prompts.md) - Instructors explicitly teach the high-frequency command and direction terms used in standardized test prompts so learners can decode what each question actually asks.
* [Teach Kids to Choose Their Self-Talk](teach_kids_to_choose_their_self-talk.md) - A guided introspection routine in which students observe their own inner speech, categorize it as positive/neutral or negative, and practice deliberately reframing negative self-talk into constructive alternatives.
* [Teach Metacognitive Strategies](teach-metacognitive-strategies.md) - Explicitly instructing learners in planning, monitoring, and evaluating their own thinking so they can regulate learning independently.
* [Teach Mindfulness Strategies](teach_mindfulness_strategies.md) - Teach students various mindfulness strategies, from meditation and deep breathing to journaling and drawing.
* [Teach Positive Actions](teach_positive_actions.md) - A comprehensive social-emotional and character development strategy that explicitly teaches positive behaviors across six domains — physical health, intellectual growth, self-management, social relationships, honesty, and self-improvement.
* [Teach Self Regulation Strategies](teach-self-regulation-strategies.md) - Explicitly teaching learners strategies for goal-setting, monitoring, and reflecting on their own learning so they can manage their cognition, motivation, and behavior.
* [Teach Self-Regulation Skills](teach_self-regulation_skills.md) - Explicitly teach learners strategies for monitoring and managing their own emotional, behavioral, and cognitive responses so they can engage productively in learning activities.
* [Teach-OK](teach-ok.md) - A fast-paced peer teaching routine in which the teacher briefly presents a concept, then pairs take turns re-teaching it to each other using gestures.
* [Teacher As Learner](teacher-as-learner.md) - Teachers deliberately re-enter the learner role — studying new content, skills, or student thinking — to deepen pedagogical content knowledge and empathy for the learner experience.
* [Teacher as Writer](teacher_as_writer.md) - To understand the writing process, teachers have to write also.
* [Teacher Modeling](teacher_modeling.md) - Demonstrating writing strategies and techniques by showing students how you use them yourself, including the struggles and revisions along the way.
* [Teacher Parent Communication](teacher-parent-communication.md) - Structured, two-way communication between teachers and families about student progress, behavior, and learning, used to align home and school support.
* [Teacher Racial Identity Work](teacher_racial_identity_work.md) - Teacher racial identity work involves educators examining their own racial identity, biases, and privilege to create a more inclusive and equitable classroom environment.
* [Teacher Read-Aloud That Models Reading for Deep Understanding](teacher_read-aloud_that_models_reading_for_deep_understanding.md) - A teacher read-aloud involves a teacher fluently reading a text aloud to learners, intentionally modeling strategies of understanding that can be reinforced in shared, guided, and independent reading.
* [Teacher-Led Physical Math Games](teacher-led_physical_math_games.md) - Teacher-led physical activities integrate movement and mathematical practice in an abbreviated time frame, suitable for station breaks and transitions, and are adaptable to different grade levels.
* [Teacher-Student Reading Conferences](teacher-student_reading_conferences.md) - Schedule regular one-on-one conferences with students to assess their independent reading, provide individualized instructional support, and document progress.
* [Teaching Academic Vocabulary](teaching_academic_vocabulary.md) - Explicitly teach discipline-specific academic vocabulary — including terms whose everyday meanings differ from their technical meanings — using visuals, context, and learners' home languages as supports.
* [Teaching Academic Vocabulary in PBL](teaching_academic_vocabulary_in_pbl.md) - In project-based learning (PBL), teaching academic vocabulary involves identifying specialized or technical words students will encounter during a project and planning explicit instruction so all learners can access and use that language.
* [Teaching For Transfer](teaching-for-transfer.md) - Designing instruction so that knowledge and skills acquired in one context are applied in new, dissimilar contexts.
* [Teaching Metacognitive Strategies](teaching-metacognitive-strategies.md) - Explicitly teaching learners to plan, monitor, and evaluate their own thinking and learning processes.
* [Teaching Social Awareness Through Current Events and Fictional Characters](teaching_social_awareness_through_current_events_and_fictional_characters.md) - Model empathy and explain different perspectives using current events and fictional characters as vehicles for social-emotional learning.
* [Team Based Learning](team-based-learning.md) - A structured whole-course strategy that uses permanent small teams and a readiness assurance cycle so most class time is spent on team application of concepts rather than direct instruction.
* [Team Contracts](team_contracts.md) - A co-created written agreement among team members that specifies roles, norms, communication practices, and accountability procedures for collaborative work.
* [Team-Building Through Problem-Based Learning](team-building_through_problem-based_learning.md) - Using problem-based learning in multi-cultural groups helps learners get used to working in a multi-cultural environment.
* [Technology Accessibility](technology_accessibility.md) - Ensuring learners have ready access to a varied set of digital and media tools — and the support to use them — so that learning activities are not limited by equipment, skills, or environment.
* [Technology Breaks](technology_breaks.md) - Taking short, scheduled breaks from technology during study periods reduces the negative effects of media multitasking.
* [Teen Exercise as Stress Management](teen_exercise_as_stress_management.md) - Encourage teens to engage in regular exercise as a way to manage stress, improve focus, and enhance overall well-being.
* [Temperature Control](temperature_control.md) - Maintaining classroom temperatures within a moderate comfort range (roughly 68–74°F / 20–23°C) to protect attention, working memory, and engagement.
* [Temperature Monitoring & Adjustment](temperature_monitoring_&_adjustment.md) - Monitoring the temperature of a learning environment and adjusting it to maintain an optimal range improves learner focus, reduces distractibility, and supports achievement.
* [Temperature Monitoring for Learning Environments](temperature_monitoring_for_learning_environments.md) - Monitoring and adjusting the thermal conditions of a learning space to keep learners in the range where attention, comfort, and performance are maximized.
* [Temperature Regulation](temperature_regulation.md) - Maintaining classroom temperatures within a moderate, comfortable range (roughly 68–74°F / 20–23°C) to protect attention, working memory, and achievement.
* [Ten Frame Activities](ten-frame-activities.md) - Structured activities using a 2×5 grid to build learners' subitizing, number sense, and early arithmetic reasoning through visual quantity representation.
* [Ten Frames](ten-frames.md) - A ten-frame is a 2×5 grid on which counters are placed to represent numbers, making the structure of numbers within 10 (and, doubled, within 20) visually explicit for early learners.
* [Test Anxiety Reduction](test-anxiety-reduction.md) - A set of instructional and assessment design practices that lower learners' evaluative anxiety so it does not impair performance or learning.
* [Test-Study-Test Technique](test-study-test_technique.md) - A spelling instruction cycle in which students take a pretest, immediately correct their errors under teacher guidance, study the corrected words, and then take a final test.
* [Text + Social Annotation + Structured Activity](text_+_social_annotation_+_structured_activity.md) - Students read shared online texts equipped with social annotation tools, discuss passages in the margins with peers, and complete a structured activity that applies what the reading and discussion surfaced.
* [Text + Structured Activity](text_+_structured_activity.md) - Students engage with text resources (physical or digital) accompanied by a structured activity designed to ensure active learning and engagement with the material.
* [Text Based Discussion Protocols](text-based-discussion-protocols.md) - Structured routines that organize whole-class or small-group discussion around a shared text, assigning roles, talk moves, and norms so that conversation builds understanding of the text rather than drifting into unstructured opinion exchange.
* [Text Based_Collaborative_Learning](text-based_collaborative_learning.md) - Learners construct understanding together through structured interaction around shared texts — discussing, annotating, questioning, and writing about what they read.
* [Text Chats in Instruction](text_chats_in_instruction.md) - Using real-time text messaging platforms to support communication, questioning, and collaboration during instruction.
* [Text Illustrations With Graphic Organizers](text_illustrations_with_graphic_organizers.md) - Learners add graphic organizers to finished expository or argumentative writing to illustrate a concept, providing a supportive diagram that aids reader comprehension.
* [Text Marking](text_marking.md) - Learners actively annotate a text — underlining, circling, labeling, or symbol-coding — to identify structure, key ideas, and relationships while reading.
* [Text Marking And Highlighting](text-marking-and-highlighting.md) - Learners selectively mark, underline, or highlight text to isolate key information for later review.
* [Text Set Construction](text-set-construction.md) - Curating a deliberately sequenced collection of texts on a single topic — varied in difficulty, genre, and perspective — so learners build knowledge incrementally through reading.
* [Text Talk](text-talk.md) - A dialogic read-aloud strategy in which teachers intersperse open-ended questioning about decontextualized storybook language, with explicit attention to Tier 2 vocabulary, to build young learners' comprehension and word knowledge.
* [Text Talk Interactive Read Aloud](text-talk-interactive-read-aloud.md) - A read-aloud protocol in which teachers intersperse open, meaning-focused questions during and after reading to build comprehension and vocabulary from rich texts.
* [Text To Speech Technology](text-to-speech-technology.md) - Text-to-speech (TTS) technology converts written text into synthesized spoken audio, allowing learners to listen to text rather than — or alongside — reading it.
* [Text To Speech Tools](text-to-speech-tools.md) - Software that converts written text into synthesized spoken audio, allowing learners to listen to digital text rather than — or alongside — reading it.
* [Text-Based Discussions](text-based_discussions.md) - Structured discussions in which learners talk about a shared text, using evidence from the text itself to support their interpretations and responses.
* [Text-to-Speech (TTS)](text-to-speech_(tts).md) - Text-to-speech (TTS) technology converts written text into spoken words, enhancing accessibility and comprehension for individuals with dyslexia, visual impairments, or other learning differences.
* [Text-to-Speech (TTS) Software](text-to-speech_(tts)_software.md) - Text-to-speech (TTS) software converts written text into spoken audio, providing an alternative access route to digital and printed content.
* [Text-to-Speech Software](text-to-speech_software.md) - Text-to-speech (TTS) software converts written text into spoken words, providing an alternative access route to text for learners with reading difficulties and a multimodal supplement for others.
* [Text-to-Text, Text-to-Self, Text-to-World](text-to-text,_text-to-self,_text-to-world.md) - A reading comprehension strategy in which learners make three types of connections to a text — to other texts, to their own experiences, and to world events — to deepen engagement and understanding.
* [The Best Part of Me Book](the_best_part_of_me_book.md) - Have the class create a book in which each student tells one thing they like about themselves.
* [The Disciplinary Steps](the_disciplinary_steps.md) - A structured, eight-step conversation for responding to learner misbehavior that separates emotional acknowledgment from consequence, applies policy consistently, and ends with an explicit expectation of future improvement.
* [The Frayer Model](the_frayer_model.md) - A graphic organizer that builds conceptual understanding of a single term by requiring a definition, essential characteristics, examples, and non-examples.
* [The Pencil Pledge](the_pencil_pledge.md) - A classroom ritual in which students publicly commit to caring for shared materials and acting responsibly toward the learning environment.
* [The Role of Background Knowledge](the_role_of_background_knowledge.md) - Learners may hold relevant experiences and knowledge that instruction must surface and connect to — or, where knowledge is missing, instruction must build it before new content can be understood.
* [The Title (Pre-Reading)](the_title_(pre-reading).md) - Learners are given the title of a reading and, in small groups, pool their prior knowledge and predictions about the topic before reading.
* [Things to Consider as a Healthy Skeptic](things_to_consider_as_a_healthy_skeptic.md) - Provide adolescents with opportunities to see the value of a healthy skepticism toward information they encounter in both online and offline contexts.
* [Think Aloud Modeling](think-aloud_modeling.md) - The instructor verbalizes their reasoning, monitoring, and decision-making while performing a task, making expert thinking visible for learners.
* [Think Time](think_time.md) - Deliberately extending the pause after questions, prompts, or reading demands so learners have adequate time to process before responding.
* [Think-Aloud Modeling](think-aloud-modeling.md) - Think-aloud modeling is a strategy in which an instructor performs a task while verbalizing the reasoning, checks, and decisions normally kept internal.
* [Think-Alouds](think-alouds.md) - Modeling reading and problem-solving by verbalizing one's thinking aloud, then having learners practice the same verbalization with peers.
* [Think-Alouds for Online Reading Strategies](think-alouds_for_online_reading_strategies.md) - Teachers model their online reading strategies through think-alouds.
* [Think-Pair-Share (Turn & Talk)](think-pair-share_(turn_&_talk).md) - A three-step cooperative discussion routine — individual thinking, paired discussion, whole-class sharing — that structures participation and rehearsal before public response.
* [Think-Time and Re-Reading](think-time_and_re-reading.md) - Sufficient time is allowed for learners to process information and re-read materials if needed.
* [Think-Write-Pair-Share](think-write-pair-share.md) - A four-stage collaborative strategy — individual thinking, written response, paired discussion, and whole-class sharing — that structures participation before discussion begins.
* [Think-Write-Share](think-write-share.md) - Learners think independently about a question, write a response, then share with peers or the class, ensuring universal engagement and surfacing misconceptions.
* [Thinking About Emotions in Advance](thinking_about_emotions_in_advance.md) - Begin instruction by prompting students to surface and name their current emotional states before academic work begins.
* [Thinking Critically](thinking_critically.md) - A strategy that develops learners' ability to question, analyze, evaluate, and reason about content through structured questioning, dialogue, and argumentation.
* [Thinking Tools with Graphic Organizers](thinking_tools_with_graphic_organizers.md) - When teachers deliver content through lectures, readings, or video, students interact with that content by completing a graphic organizer in groups, pairs, or individually.
* [Three Act Tasks](three-act-tasks.md) - A three-phase lesson structure for mathematics in which learners first experience a compelling phenomenon, then resolve it through problem solving, then compare their solution against the resolution.
* [Three-Phase Lesson](three-phase_lesson.md) - A lesson structure that sequences instruction into Before (activate and frame), During (active exploration or work), and After (consolidate and connect) phases.
* [Three-Tiered Writing Conferences](three-tiered_writing_conferences.md) - A structured format for brief one-to-one writing conferences that sequences feedback through three perspectives — personal reader, community member, and teacher — ending with student reflection and planning.
* [Through the Eyes of the People in a Story](through_the_eyes_of_the_people_in_a_story.md) - Learners pick a character from a short story or book and relive the main events from that character's point of view.
* [Tier Two Vocabulary Instruction](tier_two_vocabulary_instruction.md) - Focuses on teaching high-frequency words used by mature language users that can add significantly to language ability.
* [Tiered Assignments](tiered-assignments.md) - The same core learning goal is addressed through tasks at different levels of complexity, support, or abstraction, so all learners work on appropriately challenging versions of the same assignment.
* [Tiered Assignments](tiered_assignments.md) - Designing the same core task at multiple levels of complexity, scaffolding, or abstraction so all learners work on the same essential understanding at an appropriate challenge level.
* [Tiered Seating Areas](tiered_seating_areas.md) - A physical classroom arrangement in which seating is stepped or elevated so that every participant in a group can see and hear the speaker, supporting small-group discussion and demonstration.
* [Tiered Vocabulary Instruction](tiered-vocabulary-instruction.md) - Categorizes words into three tiers by frequency and utility, concentrating explicit instruction on high-utility Tier Two words that mature language users encounter across contexts.
* [Tiered Vocabulary Instruction](tiered_vocabulary_instruction.md) - Tiered vocabulary instruction categorizes words into three tiers based on their frequency and utility, concentrating explicit teaching on high-utility words that carry the most comprehension payoff.
* [Time Boxing](time-boxing.md) - Allocating a fixed, pre-committed block of time to a learning task and stopping when the block ends, shifting the unit of planning from task completion to time spent.
* [Time Management In Instruction](time-management-in-instruction.md) - Structuring and pacing instructional time so that available minutes are allocated to activities with the highest learning payoff.
* [Timed Fact Fluency Drills](timed-fact-fluency-drills.md) - Short, timed practice sessions in which learners retrieve basic facts (e.g., arithmetic combinations, sight words) repeatedly until recall becomes fast and automatic.
* [Timed Math Fact Drills](timed_math_fact_drills.md) - Brief, timed practice sessions in which learners retrieve basic arithmetic facts (e.g., single-digit addition, multiplication tables) repeatedly until recall becomes fast and automatic.
* [Timed Practice With Progress Monitoring](timed-practice-with-progress-monitoring.md) - Repeated short timed practice sessions on a target skill, with performance rates tracked over time so learners and instructors can see fluency growth and adjust goals.
* [Timed Retrieval Practice](timed-retrieval-practice.md) - Retrieval practice performed under a time limit, requiring learners to recall and produce information quickly rather than merely recognize it.
* [Timed Writing Sprints](timed-writing-sprints.md) - Short, fixed-duration writing sessions with a strict time limit and a low-stakes goal, used to generate text, build fluency, and bypass self-editing during drafting.
* [Timely Feedback](timely-feedback.md) - Providing feedback as soon as possible after a learner demonstrates performance, so the feedback connects directly to the action that prompted it.
* [Timely Feedback](timely_feedback.md) - Delivering feedback soon after performance so learners can still connect the information to the task, reducing error consolidation and enabling immediate correction.
* [Timely Peer Review](timely_peer_review.md) - Conducting peer reviews at multiple points in the writing process — before the due date, on the due date, and after grading — so feedback drives revision rather than ending the conversation.
* [Timers](timers.md) - Timers, both visual and auditory, are used to help learners gauge how much time remains during an activity, supporting greater attention and task completion.
* [Timers and Pacing](timers_and_pacing.md) - Using visible time limits and deliberate pacing of activities to focus attention, manage cognitive load, and sustain learner engagement.
* [Token Economies](token-economies.md) - A system in which learners earn tokens for target behaviors and exchange them for backup reinforcers, applying operant conditioning to shape classroom conduct and effort.
* [Token Economy](token-economy.md) - A token economy awards tangible or symbolic tokens for target behaviors, which learners later exchange for backup reinforcers.
* [Token Economy](token_economy.md) - A system in which learners earn tokens for target behaviors and exchange them for backup reinforcers, applying operant conditioning to shape classroom conduct and effort.
* [Tossing Activity](tossing_activity.md) - A hands-on activity that integrates physical movement — typically tossing or catching objects — with academic content, so that motor action becomes part of the learning task.
* [TPRI Diagnostic Assessment](tpri_diagnostic_assessment.md) - The TPRI (Texas Primary Reading Inventory) is a one-on-one diagnostic instrument that screens and inventories the reading development of students in kindergarten through third grade.
* [Training Handouts](training_handouts.md) - Training handouts are supplementary materials distributed during training sessions to enhance learner engagement, structure interactions, and provide a reference for key content.
* [Trait Identification and Construction](trait_identification_and_construction.md) - Learners identify animal body parts from puzzle pieces and construct real or mythical animals, grounding observation, measurement, and justification in hands-on life science work.
* [Trampoline Review](trampoline_review.md) - Learners review academic content while performing rhythmic gross-motor activity such as bouncing on a mini-trampoline, pairing physical movement with retrieval practice.
* [Translanguaging](translanguaging.md) - Leverages multilingual learners' full linguistic repertoire as a resource for learning, while explicitly teaching when to perform in the norms of a named language.
* [Translate Academic to Social Language](translate_academic_to_social_language.md) - Model how to say something in a more academic way or how to paraphrase academic texts into more conversational language.
* [Translate Report Cards Into Multiple Languages](translate_report_cards_into_multiple_languages.md) - Translating report cards and progress communications into families' home languages so that all parents can understand their child's academic progress.
* [Travel Journals](travel_journals.md) - Students maintain an ongoing journal that charts their learning across a unit, combining writing, drawings, and graphic organizers with reflective entries.
* [Turn and Talk](turn_and_talk.md) - Turn and Talk involves learners engaging in short discussions with a partner to explain what they heard.
* [Turn Around Time](turn-around_time.md) - Minimizing the delay between learner performance and the delivery of feedback so that feedback arrives while the task is still mentally active and correctable.
* [Turn-and-Talk](turn-and-talk.md) - Learners engage in short, structured discussions with a partner to rehearse, articulate, and refine what they have just learned.
* [Twelve Angry Men](twelve-angry-men.md) - Using the film *12 Angry Men* (Lumet, 1957) as a case for analyzing argumentation, persuasion, bias, and group decision-making.
* [Two Stage Exams](two-stage-exams.md) - An assessment format where students first complete an exam individually, then immediately retake the same or similar questions in small groups, with the group score partially counting.
* [Two Stage Quizzes](two-stage-quizzes.md) - A quiz taken individually first, then retaken in small groups with discussion, so learners commit to answers before negotiating them with peers.
* [Two Stars And A Wish](two-stars-and-a-wish.md) - A structured peer-feedback protocol in which reviewers name two strengths ("stars") and one suggestion for improvement ("wish") about a peer's work.
* [Two Stars and a Wish](two_stars_and_a_wish.md) - A structured peer-feedback format in which reviewers offer two specific strengths ("stars") and one actionable improvement suggestion ("wish").

#### U {: #letter-u }

* [Uncluttered Environment](uncluttered_environment.md) - A learning environment with minimal visual distractions is created by storing supplies and materials off of tables and desks and limiting decorations and other things hanging on the walls.
* [Uncluttered Learning Environment](uncluttered_learning_environment.md) - A clean, organized, and uncluttered learning environment is free from distractions and allows the focus to be on learning.
* [Understand Your Bias](understand_your_bias.md) - A reflective activity that surfaces learners' personal stereotypes and prejudices so they can recognize how their own viewpoint shapes their perception of others.
* [Understanding By Design Unit Template](understanding-by-design-unit-template.md) - A backward-design planning template that structures a unit around desired learning results, acceptable evidence of understanding, and only then the learning plan.
* [Ungrading](ungrading.md) - Removing or de-emphasizing letter grades and numeric scores in favor of feedback, self-assessment, and revision-oriented evaluation.
* [Unit Planning with Graphic Organizers](unit_planning_with_graphic_organizers.md) - When introducing a unit, show students how the parts of the unit fit together with a graphic organizer, giving them a map of where they are at any point and why each part matters.
* [Untimed Tests](untimed_tests.md) - Untimed tests remove the pressure of completing assessments within a set time limit, allowing learners to focus on problem-solving without time-related anxiety.
* [Use "I" Statements to Avoid Blame](use_"i"_statements_to_avoid_blame.md) - Avoid using "you" statements, which can make students defensive; instead frame feedback and conflict talk around the speaker's own observations and feelings.
* [Use Advance Organizers](use-advance-organizers.md) - Present a brief, higher-level framework (verbal, graphic, or comparative) before new material so learners can anchor incoming information to existing knowledge structures.
* [Use Analogies](use-analogies.md) - Introduce new concepts by mapping them onto familiar, well-structured knowledge domains so learners can reason from the known to the unknown.
* [Use Analogies](use_analogies.md) - Introduce new material by mapping it onto a familiar, well-understood domain so learners can reason about the unfamiliar through the familiar.
* [Use Body Language and Demonstrations](use_body_language_and_demonstrations.md) - Reinforce verbal instructions with body language, gestures, and physical demonstrations so learners can observe the target performance rather than infer it from words alone.
* [Use Books to Teach Emotions](use_books_to_teach_emotions.md) - Stories are used as examples of handling emotions in a responsible way.
* [Use Concrete Examples](use_concrete_examples.md) - Ground abstract concepts, rules, and procedures in specific, vivid instances that learners can inspect before generalizing.
* [Use Current Events & Fictional Characters](use_current_events_&_fictional_characters.md) - Grounding instruction in current events or fictional characters and narratives so learners encounter content through vivid, socially meaningful contexts.
* [Use Current Events And Fictional Characters To Teach Social Awareness](use_current_events_and_fictional_characters_to_teach_social_awareness.md) - Grounding social-emotional learning in real news events and fictional narratives so learners analyze perspectives, emotions, and ethical dilemmas through concrete characters and situations.
* [Use Estimation and Prediction](use_estimation_and_prediction.md) - Students predict their performance or task completion before working, then compare predictions against actual outcomes to calibrate self-assessment.
* [Use Formative Feedback](use-formative-feedback.md) - Provide learners with ongoing, low-stakes information about their performance that they can act on before final evaluation.
* [Use Mastery Learning](use_mastery_learning.md) - Organize instruction so learners must demonstrate criterion-level mastery of each unit before advancing, with time varying and achievement held constant.
* [Use Multiple Representations](use_multiple_representations.md) - Present key concepts in more than one representational format (e.g., verbal, visual, symbolic, concrete) so learners build richer, more flexible understanding.
* [Use Positive Affirmations](use_positive_affirmations.md) - Replace negative self-talk with positive affirmations to support self-concept, confidence, and persistence.
* [Use Pre Questions](use-pre-questions.md) - Asking learners questions about material before instruction, even when they answer incorrectly, to enhance subsequent learning of that material.
* [Use Quotations to Cite Textual Evidence](use_quotations_to_cite_textual_evidence.md) - Teaching students to select, embed, and explain direct quotes from texts so their claims are supported by evidence.
* [Use Retrieval Practice](use_retrieval_practice.md) - Learners actively recall information from memory rather than re-reading or re-watching it, strengthening retention and transfer.
* [Use Rubrics And Exemplars](use-rubrics-and-exemplars.md) - Providing learners with explicit evaluation criteria (rubrics) alongside models of high-quality work (exemplars) so they understand what success looks like before producing their own.
* [Use Spaced Practice](use_spaced_practice.md) - Distribute learning sessions and review opportunities over time rather than massing them together, to strengthen retention and transfer.
* [Use Structured Critique Protocols](use_structured_critique_protocols.md) - A strategy that replaces vague "give feedback" instructions with explicit, sequenced protocols (e.g., gallery walk, ladder of feedback, TAG, critical friends) that specify what to evaluate, in what order, and how to phrase it.
* [Use Templates and Tools That Allow for Scaffolding](use_templates_and_tools_that_allow_for_scaffolding.md) - Providing structured supports — planning templates, graphic organizers, and structured tools — that guide learners through complex tasks and are gradually removed as proficiency develops.
* [Use this Toolkit: Mitigating Racial Bias in Edtech](use_this_toolkit-mitigating_racial_bias_in_edtech.md) - A structured toolkit guiding edtech teams through identifying and mitigating racial bias across the full product design and development lifecycle.
* [Use Visual Aids](use_visual_aids.md) - Supplement verbal instructions with visual aids to support learners' understanding, especially for low-level students.
* [Use Worked Examples](use_worked_examples.md) - The instructor presents a fully solved problem — showing each step and explaining the reasoning behind it — and then asks learners to solve a similar problem independently or in pairs.
* [User-Friendly Library Website Practices](user-friendly_library_website_practices.md) - Designing library websites so students can efficiently find and use resources through clear navigation, plain language, accessibility, and usability testing.
* [Using a Culturally Responsive Curriculum Scorecard](using_a_culturally_responsive_curriculum_scorecard.md) - A collaborative audit tool in which diverse stakeholder teams systematically score curriculum materials for representation, social justice content, and culturally responsive teaching practices.
* [Using Colors for Different Feelings](using_colors_for_different_feelings.md) - Learners use different colors to represent different emotions in art, creating associations between emotions and visual cues that support emotional expression and awareness.
* [Using Empathy Interviews for Family Engagement](using_empathy_interviews_for_family_engagement.md) - Empathy interviews are short, one-on-one, open-ended conversations with families designed to surface their lived experiences, values, and concerns so that school decisions are grounded in authentic community voice.
* [Using Manipulatives](using_manipulatives.md) - Manipulatives are physical objects used as teaching tools to engage students in hands-on learning, especially in mathematics.
* [Using Positive Affirmations](using_positive_affirmations.md) - Replacing negative self-talk with positive affirmations to support self-efficacy, emotional regulation, and a growth mindset.
* [Using Reflection for Habit Formation](using_reflection_for_habit_formation.md) - Structured reflective routines that turn episodic learning experiences into durable habits of meaning-making, self-monitoring, and transfer.
* [Using Technology](using_technology.md) - Integrating digital tools and media into instruction in ways that support — rather than distract from — evidence-based learning processes.
* [Utility Value Connections](utility-value-connections.md) - A motivational strategy in which learners articulate or receive explicit connections between course content and their own lives, goals, or interests.
* [Utilize Text Messaging for Parent Engagement](utilize_text_messaging_for_parent_engagement.md) - Text messaging is used as a primary, low-friction channel for educators to communicate regularly with parents, prompting small at-home learning behaviors.
* [Utilizing Mobile Communication Apps for Parent Engagement](utilizing_mobile_communication_apps_for_parent_engagement.md) - Mobile communication apps (e.g., ClassDojo, Remind, Seesaw) let educators send texts, media summaries, and alerts to parents about school activities and their child's progress, lowering the barriers to sustained family–school communication.

#### V {: #letter-v }

* [Validate Feelings](validate_feelings.md) - Explicitly acknowledge that a learner's emotional response is real and legitimate, separating acceptance of the feeling from judgment of the behavior.
* [Value Affirmation](value-affirmation.md) - A brief writing or reflection activity in which learners identify and elaborate on personally important values, buffering identity threat and protecting learning performance.
* [Value Affirmation Before Assessment](value-affirmation-before-assessment.md) - A brief writing activity in which learners identify and elaborate on personally important values before a high-stakes evaluation, buffering threat and protecting performance.
* [Valuing the Learning Process](valuing_the_learning_process.md) - Emphasizing understanding, exploration, and growth over grades and performance outcomes, so learners orient toward mastery rather than external evaluation.
* [Varying Irrelevant Attributes in Examples](varying_irrelevant_attributes_in_examples.md) - When teaching concepts, use examples in which the irrelevant attributes vary widely.
* [Varying Levels of Choice in Assessments](varying_levels_of_choice_in_assessments.md) - Give students a choice about the areas of focus in a paper or project.
* [Venn Diagrams](venn_diagrams.md) - Venn diagrams are visual organizers that emphasize comparisons and contrasts between two or more items or concepts.
* [Verbal Feedback on Writing](verbal_feedback_on_writing.md) - Verbal feedback replaces or supplements written margin comments with recorded spoken commentary that students listen to, then use to revise their work.
* [Verbal Repetition](verbal_repetition.md) - Learners repeat, restate, or rehearse information aloud to strengthen retention and make their understanding observable.
* [Video Based Instruction](video-based-instruction.md) - Using video as the primary medium for delivering instructional content, typically combining narration with visual demonstration.
* [Video Based Modeling](video-based-modeling.md) - Using video recordings of expert performance to show learners how a skill, process, or procedure is executed before they attempt it themselves.
* [Video Report Cards](video_report_cards.md) - Sending parents a link to a personalized video that provides context about their child's report card, such as a history of the child's performance in core subjects, areas for growth, and recommendations to further support the child's learning.
* [Videoconferencing for Collaboration](videoconferencing_for_collaboration.md) - Videoconferencing platforms with built-in collaboration features (Microsoft Teams, Google Meet, Zoom) used to replicate and support teamwork experiences in online and hybrid learning.
* [Videos](videos.md) - Videos are dynamic resources for supporting curricula, engaging students through sight and sound.
* [Videos (Pre-Reading)](videos_(pre-reading).md) - A short video on the topic of an upcoming reading is shown before reading, followed by structured peer discussion, to activate prior knowledge and build context.
* [Videos: SEL Topics](videos-sel_topics.md) - Using short video clips on SEL topics followed by discussions and/or related activities promotes relevance and meaningful connection of these topics to learners' background knowledge and personal lives.
* [Virtual Embodiment](virtual_embodiment.md) - Virtual embodiment uses immersive VR body ownership illusions to let learners temporarily experience the world from another person's body and social position.
* [Virtual Flipped Classroom](virtual_flipped_classroom.md) - A fully online variant of the flipped classroom in which all instruction is delivered asynchronously and synchronous contact is reserved for individualized tutoring and office hours.
* [Virtual Reality (VR) for Empathy Training](virtual_reality_(vr)_for_empathy_training.md) - Learners inhabit a first-person virtual scenario (e.g., a customer in financial crisis) to build perspective-taking and empathic response skills through embodied experience, practice, and feedback.
* [Virtual Reality (VR) Skills Management Assessments](virtual_reality_(vr)_skills_management_assessments.md) - Immersive VR simulations are used to place employees in high-pressure customer scenarios (e.g., Black Friday crowds, angry shoppers) so their responses can be observed, scored, and used for development and promotion decisions.
* [Virtual Reality (VR) Training](virtual_reality_(vr)_training.md) - VR training places learners inside simulated, interactive 3D environments where they can rehearse procedures and decisions safely before performing them in real settings.
* [Virtual Reality Immersive Training](virtual-reality-immersive-training.md) - Learners practice skills inside a computer-generated, head-tracked 3D environment that simulates the target performance context.
* [Virtual Reality Simulations](virtual-reality-simulations.md) - Immersive, computer-generated environments that let learners enact procedures, explore phenomena, or rehearse decisions in a safe, controlled replica of a real setting.
* [Virtual Reality Training](virtual-reality-training.md) - Immersive, computer-simulated environments in which learners rehearse skills and procedures through embodied interaction rather than observation.
* [Visual Cues](visual_cues.md) - Pairing spoken or written information with graphics, diagrams, or gestures to support comprehension, attention, and retention.
* [Visual Mnemonics](visual_mnemonics.md) - Visual mnemonics involve creating visual images to cue a strategy or represent a concept.
* [Visual Organizers](visual_organizers.md) - Providing or prompting learners to create spatial-graphic representations (maps, matrices, diagrams) that make the structure of content visible.
* [Visual Prompts for Essay Writing](visual_prompts_for_essay_writing.md) - Using artwork paired with life-lesson statements as timed writing prompts to help adolescents generate and elaborate essay ideas.
* [Visual Reading Aids](visual_reading_aids.md) - Physical or digital tools — reading guides, colored overlays, tracking rulers, and finger/pointer tracking — that reduce visual stress and support place-keeping and line tracking during reading.
* [Visual Representations in Mathematics](visual_representations_in_mathematics.md) - Using schematic diagrams, number lines, strip diagrams, and other visuals to depict mathematical quantities and relationships so learners can reason about abstract concepts and solve problems.
* [Visual Schedules](visual-schedules.md) - A visual schedule presents the sequence of activities, steps, or transitions in an image- or symbol-based format so learners can anticipate, navigate, and self-manage their time.
* [Visual Supports](visual-supports.md) - Visual supports use images, diagrams, graphic organizers, and other visual representations to complement or structure verbal information and reduce cognitive load.
* [Visual Timers](visual_timers.md) - Visual timers make elapsed and remaining time visible, supporting time concepts, transitions, and on-task behavior for learners who struggle with abstract time.
* [Visualizations and Mind Maps](visualizations_and_mind_maps.md) - Use visuals such as infographics, diagrams, and mind maps to transform abstract concepts into clear, structured formats that show how elements connect.
* [Visualizing](visualizing.md) - Visualizing involves creating a mental image or external representation — a picture, diagram, table, or sketch — of a problem or text to aid understanding and problem solving.
* [Vocabulary In Context](vocabulary-in-context.md) - Learners acquire word meanings by inferring them from meaningful reading or listening contexts, supported by multiple varied encounters rather than isolated definition study.
* [Vocabulary Instruction](vocabulary-instruction.md) - Explicitly teaching target words and their meanings — with definitions, context, and repeated use — so students can understand and deploy new vocabulary in reading, writing, and discussion.
* [Vocabulary Instruction](vocabulary_instruction.md) - Explicitly teaching vocabulary words and their definitions, along with providing context, to help students understand and use new words effectively.
* [Vocabulary Instruction Small Groups](vocabulary-instruction-small-groups.md) - Small-group instruction that teaches high-value vocabulary through explicit explanation, multiple encounters, and collaborative word use.
* [Vocabulary Journals](vocabulary-journals.md) - Learners maintain a personal, ongoing record of target words with definitions, contexts, and self-generated elaborations, revisited over time.
* [Vocabulary Meaning Through Context](vocabulary_meaning_through_context.md) - Instructors present vocabulary embedded in meaningful context so learners infer meaning themselves, aiding understanding and retention.
* [Vocabulary Pre Teaching](vocabulary-pre-teaching.md) - Teaching key word meanings before learners encounter them in a text or task, so that unfamiliar vocabulary does not block comprehension.
* [Vocabulary Pre-Teaching](vocabulary_pre-teaching.md) - Selecting and directly teaching a small set of high-value words before learners encounter them in a text or task, so that unfamiliar vocabulary does not block comprehension.

#### W {: #letter-w }

* [Wait Time](wait-time.md) - A deliberate pause of at least three seconds after posing a question (and again after a response) that gives learners time to process, formulate, and extend their answers.
* [Wait Time](wait_time.md) - Wait time is the period of silence between the time a question is asked and the time when one or more learners respond to that question.
* [Wall Calendar](wall_calendar.md) - A large, centrally located calendar that makes family or classroom schedules visible, helping learners see how their commitments fit into a shared timeline and build time-management habits.
* [Warm Demander Pedagogy](warm-demander-pedagogy.md) - A teaching stance that pairs high expectations and insistence on rigorous work with strong relational support, communicating belief in every learner's capacity to succeed.
* [Warning and Consequence](warning_and_consequence.md) - A classroom management technique in which the teacher states a clear behavioral expectation, warns that a specific consequence will follow if it is not met, and then consistently applies the stated consequence.
* [Waste Reduction Solutions](waste_reduction_solutions.md) - Learners examine and sort real waste items, categorize and chart them, and propose evidence-based solutions to reduce solid waste.
* [Watch Emotion Videos](watch_emotion_videos.md) - Share short, well-chosen videos that explain big emotions to children, then debrief so learners can apply regulation steps to their own experience.
* [Web Annotation Tools](web_annotation_tools.md) - Learners use free web-based tools to annotate online documents, including slideshows, blog posts, or PDFs.
* [Web-Based Dictionary & Thesaurus](web-based_dictionary_&_thesaurus.md) - Embedding online dictionaries and thesauruses into vocabulary lessons lets learners quickly look up unfamiliar words and explore word relationships at the moment of need.
* [Web-Search Scavenger Hunt](web-search_scavenger_hunt.md) - A structured activity in which students answer questions by locating and evaluating online sources, building search and information-literacy skills through guided practice.
* [Website Domain Analysis](website_domain_analysis.md) - Learners are taught the differences between .com, .gov, .org, and .edu domains to assess the reliability of websites.
* [Website Evaluation Checklist](website_evaluation_checklist.md) - Learners examine websites using a structured checklist to assess corroboration, source reliability, currency, documentation, advocacy, and conflicts of interest.
* [Weeding](weeding.md) - Systematically removing extraneous words, images, audio, and features from learning materials so that working-memory resources are devoted to essential content.
* [Weekly Student Reflection](weekly_student_reflection.md) - Students spend 10–15 minutes each week responding to structured prompts about their learning, building metacognitive awareness, self-regulation, and teacher–student dialogue.
* [Welcome Messages](welcome-messages.md) - A personal, orientation-focused message from the instructor at the start of a course that establishes social presence, clarifies expectations, and signals belonging.
* [What Would Their Day Look Like?](what_would_their_day_look_like?.md) - Learners select a photo of a person or animal in an unfamiliar situation and imagine what their daily life might be like.
* [What's My Emotion? Game](what's_my_emotion?_game.md) - Using an EQ Feeling Chart, students identify how they are feeling individually, then one student demonstrates an emotion non-verbally while the class guesses what it is.
* [What’s Below the Surface? (Iceberg Analysis)](what’s_below_the_surface?_(iceberg_analysis).md) - Uses an iceberg graphic to push students from surface-level plot events to deeper analysis of character traits, motivations, and identity.
* [What’s My Emotion? Game](what’s_my_emotion?_game.md) - One learner demonstrates an emotion through body language and facial expressions while the rest of the class guesses the emotion, building emotional literacy through enactment and feedback.
* [What’s My Number?](what’s_my_number?.md) - A guessing game where one person thinks of a number and provides clues, and others use logical reasoning and deduction to determine the number.
* [Where Are the People Like Me?](where_are_the_people_like_me?.md) - Learners examine media examples to consider who is represented and who is not, then explore the impact of this lack of diversity and how it might affect those who are misrepresented or not represented.
* [Which One Doesn’t Belong?](which_one_doesn’t_belong?.md) - Learners compare a small set of cards representing quantities and identify — and justify — which one differs, building number sense through structured comparison.
* [Whole Class Self-Monitoring](whole_class_self-monitoring.md) - Whole class self-monitoring involves the entire class participating in self-monitoring techniques, adapted from individual self-monitoring methods.
* [Whole Task Practice](whole-task-practice.md) - Learners practice complete, authentic versions of the target task from the start, rather than isolated subskills practiced separately before being combined.
* [Wide Reading](wide-reading.md) - Sustained, high-volume reading of self-selected, accessible texts to build fluency, vocabulary, background knowledge, and motivation.
* [Windows, Mirrors, and Sliding Glass Doors](windows,_mirrors,_and_sliding_glass_doors.md) - This strategy involves using stories and materials that act as mirrors, windows, and sliding glass doors to help students identify their place in the world.
* [Wise Feedback](wise-feedback.md) - Feedback that combines high standards with assured support, framed to signal that the giver believes the learner can improve.
* [Withitness](withitness.md) - The teacher's communicated awareness of everything happening in the classroom at all times, which prevents misbehavior and sustains engagement.
* [Word Building](word_building.md) - Word building is a literacy strategy in which learners manipulate letter tiles or squares to construct words, reinforcing phonemic awareness, letter-sound correspondence, spelling, and word recognition.
* [Word Detective (Word Identification In Context)](word_detective_(word_identification_in_context).md) - Learners search for and identify target vocabulary words within varied authentic contexts such as song lyrics, sentences, and familiar texts.
* [Word Family Charts](word-family-charts.md) - A word study strategy that organizes words sharing a common rime or spelling pattern into visible chart groups, helping learners decode and spell by analogy.
* [Word Maps](word_maps.md) - A word map is a visual organizer that promotes vocabulary development by having learners elaborate a target word across multiple dimensions — definition, synonyms, antonyms, examples, and imagery.
* [Word Of The Week](word-of-the-week.md) - A routine in which one high-value word is introduced, deeply explored, and repeatedly revisited across a week to build durable vocabulary knowledge.
* [Word Prediction Software](word-prediction-software.md) - Software that suggests words as a learner types, reducing spelling and transcription demands so composing effort can go to ideas and structure.
* [Word Problem Creation](word_problem_creation.md) - Learners select math operations and create multistep word problems for peers to solve, integrating math and literacy skills.
* [Word Processing and Technology Tools](word_processing_and_technology_tools.md) - Use word processors and other digital writing tools to support drafting, revising, and producing written work.
* [Word Processing for Composition](word_processing_for_composition.md) - Using word processing software to support drafting, revising, and feedback in writing instruction.
* [Word Processing for Writing Skills](word_processing_for_writing_skills.md) - Using word processing software such as Google Docs allows learners to draft, share, review, and revise their writing, with built-in features supporting feedback, revision, and diverse learner needs.
* [Word Sort](word-sort.md) - Students categorize key terms into self-generated groups before formal definitions are provided, then revisit and revise their sorts after instruction.
* [Word Sort](word_sort.md) - Students sort key terms into categories before instruction, then re-sort after exposure to definitions, making their prior knowledge visible and building relational frameworks.
* [Word Sorting](word-sorting.md) - Learners categorize words by shared orthographic or phonological features, inducing spelling patterns and sound–symbol relationships through comparison rather than explicit rule memorization.
* [Word Sorts](word-sorts.md) - A hands-on word study activity in which learners categorize words by shared orthographic, phonological, or morphological features to build decoding, spelling, and vocabulary knowledge.
* [Word Sorts](word_sorts.md) - Learners categorize word cards by shared orthographic, phonological, or morphological features, inducing spelling-sound patterns through comparison rather than memorizing rules.
* [Word Study](word-study.md) - An investigative approach to spelling and word knowledge instruction in which students examine, sort, and compare words to discover patterns and generalizations rather than memorize word lists.
* [Word Study](word_study.md) - Word study is an approach to spelling and word knowledge instruction that emphasizes active investigation of word patterns rather than rote memorization of word lists.
* [Word Study Developmental Spelling](word-study-developmental-spelling.md) - Word study organizes spelling instruction around learners' developmental stages of orthographic knowledge, using word sorts and contrastive word sets rather than memorization of arbitrary lists.
* [Word Walls](word-walls.md) - A prominently displayed, growing collection of key content vocabulary that learners access, use, and reorganize throughout instruction to move words into long-term memory.
* [Word Walls](word_walls.md) - Word walls are a strategy for supporting vocabulary development and retention by providing learners with constant access to important content vocabulary.
* [Worked Example Fading](worked-example-fading.md) - A strategy that progressively transitions learners from studying fully worked examples to completing partial solutions to solving problems independently.
* [Worked Example Routine](worked_example_routine.md) - A structured instructional routine — Get ready, Read, Discuss, Explain, You Try — that converts worked examples from passive reading into active engagement with expert solution reasoning.
* [Worked Examples](worked-examples.md) - Presenting fully solved problems with step-by-step reasoning for learners to study before attempting similar problems themselves.
* [Worked Examples First](worked-examples-first.md) - Presenting fully worked solutions before asking learners to solve problems independently, so novices study expert performance instead of searching for it.
* [Worked Examples Types](worked_examples_types.md) - Worked example types range from fully worked modelling examples (expert narrates every step and why) through completion problems and case studies, forming a guidance continuum from maximum support toward independent problem solving.
* [Worked_Examples_First](worked_examples_first.md) - Sequence instruction so learners study fully worked solutions before attempting problems themselves, then fade support as expertise grows.
* [Working Memory Support Strategies](working-memory-support-strategies.md) - A family of design moves that reduce, segment, or externalize the working-memory demands of instruction so limited capacity is spent on learning rather than on holding intermediate information.
* [Working Out Loud](working_out_loud.md) - Learners make their in-progress work, questions, and reasoning visible to a network or community, inviting feedback and building relationships around learning.
* [Workshop-Based Writing Instruction](workshop-based_writing_instruction.md) - A structured instructional format in which learners draft, receive peer and teacher feedback, revise, and publish writing within a predictable workshop routine.
* [Write Alouds](write_alouds.md) - Write-aloud lessons, also known as modeled writing, involve an experienced writer (typically the teacher) verbalizing their thought process while writing.
* [Write with a Transition Handout](write_with_a_transition_handout.md) - Before learners write, provide a handout of transitions, model where they fit, and explain how they guide the reader.
* [Write Your Own Word Problem](write_your_own_word_problem.md) - For homework, assign learners the task of writing some of their own word problems containing some of the key words discussed in class.
* [Writing Across The Curriculum](writing-across-the-curriculum.md) - Writing Across The Curriculum (WAC) embeds writing tasks in all subject areas so students use writing to learn content, not just to demonstrate it.
* [Writing Conferences](writing-conferences.md) - One-on-one, conversation-based meetings in which a teacher and student discuss the student's writing in progress, positioning the teacher as coach rather than evaluator.
* [Writing Conferences](writing_conferences.md) - Writing conferences are brief, individualized meetings between a teacher and a student focused on the student's writing.
* [Writing Down Goals](writing_down_goals.md) - Writing down goals makes them tangible, supports prioritization, and increases commitment through externalized, reviewable commitments.
* [Writing For Real Audiences](writing-for-real-audiences.md) - Having students write for genuine readers beyond the teacher, with authentic purposes, so rhetorical decisions become meaningful and feedback comes from real responses.
* [Writing Process Approach](writing_process_approach.md) - A process-oriented approach to writing instruction that treats writing as recursive planning, drafting, and revising, situated in a collaborative classroom community with authentic audiences and purposes.
* [Writing Process Portfolios](writing-process-portfolios.md) - Students collect, revise, and reflect on writing drafts over time, making their compositional process visible and assessed rather than only the final product.
* [Writing To Learn](writing-to-learn.md) - Using writing tasks as a thinking tool — short, low-stakes writing that helps learners process, connect, and consolidate content rather than demonstrate polished composition skill.
* [Writing Workshop](writing_workshop.md) - A structured instructional format in which students write for sustained periods, receive explicit mini-lessons, and engage in peer conferencing and teacher conferences within a predictable workshop routine.
* [Writing Workshops](writing_workshops.md) - Writing workshops create environments with multiple stations focusing on different aspects of writing, such as transitions, topic sentences, or source integration.
* [Written Feedback](written-feedback.md) - Providing learners with written comments on their work that indicate quality, gaps, and next steps.
* [WWYD Critical Incidents](wwyd_critical_incidents.md) - Learners analyze short, ambiguous conflict scenarios and answer "What would you do if you were person X/person Y?", forcing perspective-taking and reasoned resolution before discussion.

#### Y {: #letter-y }

* [Yoga, Breathing, And Meditation](yoga,_breathing,_and_meditation.md) - 将瑜伽、呼吸和冥想练习融入教育环境，以调节觉醒状态、支持自我调节，并为学习做好身心准备。
* [Youth Participatory Action Research (YPAR)](youth_participatory_action_research_(ypar).md) - Youth Participatory Action Research (YPAR) is an approach where young people collaborate with adults to conduct research and implement actions to address issues in their communities.

#### Z {: #letter-z }

* [Zoom Family Hours](zoom_family_hours.md) - Hold evening office or family hours on Zoom for parents and family members to pop in and ask questions or otherwise engage with you about their children.
* [Zoom In](zoom-in.md) - A visual inquiry routine in which learners examine a small portion of an image before seeing the whole, building and revising hypotheses as new evidence is revealed.

#### 0-9 & Other {: #letter-num }

* ['What's My Emotion?' Game Check-In]('what's_my_emotion?'_game_check-in.md) - Students use the EQ Feeling Chart to identify, act out, and guess feelings, building emotional literacy through a game-based check-in.
* [...](leveraging_strengths_in_relationship_skills.md) - ...
* [3 2 1_Reflection](3-2-1_reflection.md) - A structured closure routine in which learners record 3 things they learned, 2 things they found interesting or want to know more about, and 1 question or lingering confusion.
* [3-Source Rule](3-source_rule.md) - A corroboration strategy requiring students to confirm any claim found online against at least three independent sources before accepting or citing it.
* [5-Minute Writing Conferences](5-minute_writing_conferences.md) - Brief, structured one-on-one conferences in which students lead with a self-assessment and the teacher delivers targeted, rubric-anchored feedback on one strength and one growth area.

* [\"Document Outline\" Tool]("document_outline"_tool.md) - Students use the \"Document outline\" tool in Google Docs to extract evidence supporting the main ideas they've found in a text.
* [\"Headings and Highlight\" Strategy]("headings_and_highlight"_strategy.md) - A collaborative digital reading routine in which pairs highlight key ideas and distill sections into four-word headings, then compare and justify their choices with text evidence.
